\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,t}}^{2}+cT\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|\rho\|_{L^{q,\infty}_{x,t}}^{\frac{2\theta q}{d(1-\theta)}},

by taking the Young’s inequality with
(1-\theta)+\theta=1
and applying the mass conservation property. Hence the combination of (
4.5
) and (
4.6
) yield (
4.3
) for
1<q<m
.

If
q=m
, then we observe that
{\lambda}_{q}=2
and

\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{2}\rho=\left(\frac{2m}{2m-1}\right)^{2}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{2m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}.

For any
1<p<2
, Young’s inequality with
\frac{p}{2}+\frac{2-p}{2}=1
gives

\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{p}\rho\leq\varepsilon\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{2}\rho+c(\varepsilon)\rho,

that completes the proof of (
4.3
) by taking integration on
Q_{T}
.

Now we prove (
4.4
). First, by taking the Hölder inequality with
\frac{{\lambda}}{q_{1}}+\frac{q_{1}-{\lambda}}{q_{1}}=1
for
q_{1}>{\lambda}
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|V|^{{\lambda}}\rho\,dx\leq\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}^{{\lambda}}\|\rho\|_{L^{r_{1}}_{x}}\quad\text{where}\quad r_{1}:=\frac{q_{1}}{q_{1}-{\lambda}}.

(4.7)

Let us set

q\leq r_{1}\leq\frac{d(q+m-1)}{d-2}\quad\text{ and}\quad\theta:=\frac{q}{r_{1}}\left(1-\frac{(r_{1}-q)(d-2)}{2q+d(m-1)}\right).

(4.8)

Then by (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.4
, we yield, from (
4.7
), that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|V|^{{\lambda}}\rho\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}^{{\lambda}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{\theta}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2(1-\theta)}{q+m-1}}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+c(\varepsilon)\|\rho\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{\frac{\theta(q+m-1)}{q+m-1-(1-\theta)}}\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}^{\frac{{\lambda}(q+m-1)}{q+m-1-(1-\theta)}},

by taking Young’s inequality with
\frac{1-\theta}{q+m-1}+\frac{q+m-1-(1-\theta)}{q+m-1}=1
. Then now let us set

q_{2}:=\frac{{\lambda}(q+m-1)}{q+m-1-(1-\theta)}=\frac{q_{1}{\lambda}(2q+d(m-1))}{q_{1}(2q+d(m+q-2))-dq{\lambda}}.

(4.10)

Then the direct computation gives that the pairs
(q_{1},q_{2})
satisfy
\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
. Moreover, from (
4.8
) and (
4.10
), we deduce that

\frac{d{\lambda}(q+m-1)}{2+d(q+m-2)}\leq q_{1}\leq\frac{q{\lambda}}{q-1}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\lambda}\leq q_{2}\leq\frac{{\lambda}(q+m-1)}{q+m-2}.

Moreover, by taking integration in terms of
t
to (
4.9
), it gives

\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{{\lambda}}\rho\,dx\,dt\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,t}}^{2}+c(\varepsilon)\|\rho\|_{L^{q,\infty}_{x,t}}^{\frac{\theta q_{2}}{{\lambda}}}\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}.

(4.11)

For any
1<p<{\lambda}
, we take the Hölder inequality with
\frac{p}{{\lambda}}+\frac{{\lambda}-p}{{\lambda}}=1
and apply the mass conservation property

\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{p}\rho\,dx\,dt\leq\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{{\lambda}}\rho\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{p}{{\lambda}}}\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{{\lambda}-p}{{\lambda}}}\leq T^{\frac{{\lambda}-p}{{\lambda}}}\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{{\lambda}}\rho\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{p}{{\lambda}}}.

(4.12)

Then by taking Young’s inequality to the combined inequality of (
4.12
) and (
4.11
), we obtain (
4.4
).

Recalling
p
-th power
\langle x\rangle^{p}=\left(1+|x|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}
for
1<p\leq 2
, we note the following properties:

\nabla\langle x\rangle^{p}=p\,x\left(1+|x|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\quad\text{so}\quad\left|\nabla\langle x\rangle^{p}\right|<p\langle x\rangle^{p-1},

(4.13)

and

\Delta\langle x\rangle^{p}=p\frac{1+(p-1)|x|^{2}}{\left(1+|x|^{2}\right)^{2-p/2}}<p\frac{1+|x|^{2}}{\left(1+|x|^{2}\right)^{2-p/2}}=p\left(1+|x|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p-2}{2}}\leq p.

(4.14)

In the following lemma, we compute
p
-th moment estimate assuming that
V
satisfies (
4.2
).

Lemma 4.3

(
p
-th moment estimate)
Let
m>1
,
1\leq q\leq m
, and
1<p\leq{\lambda_{q}}:=1+\frac{d(q-1)+q}{d(m-1)+q}
. Suppose that
\rho:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
is narrowly continuous, and is a regular solution with sufficiently fast decay at infinity in spatial variables of (
1.1
). If
V\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
satisfies (
4.2
), then it holds

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\rho(\cdot,t)\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt

(4.15)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt+c_{1}T+c_{2}T^{\beta_{1}\frac{{\lambda_{q}}-p}{{\lambda_{q}}}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q,\infty}_{x,t}}^{\beta\beta_{1}\frac{qp(q_{1}-{\lambda_{q}})}{q_{1}{\lambda_{q}}}}\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}}^{p\beta_{1}},

where
c_{1}=c_{1}(\varepsilon,p)
,
c_{2}=c_{2}(\varepsilon,m,d,p,q)
,
\beta=1-\frac{(d-2)(q_{1}(1-q)+q{\lambda_{q}})}{(q_{1}-{\lambda_{q}})(d(m-1)+2q)}
, and
\beta_{1}=\frac{q_{2}{\lambda_{q}}}{q_{2}({\lambda_{q}}-p)+p{\lambda_{q}}}
.

Proof.
For simplicity, let
\lambda:=\lambda_{q}
.
By testing
\langle x\rangle^{p}
for
p>1
to (
1.1
) and taking the integration by parts, we obtain

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[\Delta\rho^{m}-\nabla\left(\rho V\right)\right]\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[\rho^{m}\Delta\langle x\rangle^{p}+\rho V\cdot\nabla\langle x\rangle^{p}\right]\,dx.

By taking the integration in
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx+\underbrace{\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\rho^{m}\Delta\langle x\rangle^{p}\right|\,dx\,dt}_{:=J_{1}}+\underbrace{\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\rho V\cdot\nabla\langle x\rangle^{p}\right|\,dx\,dt}_{:=J_{2}}.

(4.16)

Let us set

m=(1-\theta)+\frac{d(q+m-1)}{d-2}\theta\quad\iff\quad\theta=\frac{(m-1)(d-2)}{d(q+m-1)-(d-2)}\in(0,1).

Then by (
4.14
), Hölder inequality, and Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq p\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho^{m}\,dx\,dt\leq p\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\,dx\right)^{1-\theta}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{\frac{d(q+m-1)}{d-2}}\,dx\right)^{\theta}\,dt

(4.17)

\displaystyle\leq p\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\,dx\right)^{1-\theta}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\right)^{\frac{\theta d}{d-2}}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt+c(p,\varepsilon)T\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{d(q-1)+2m}{d(q-1)+2}},

by mass conservation property and Young’s inequality for
\frac{\theta d}{d-2}+\frac{d(1-\theta)-2}{d-2}=1
.

Now let us handle
J_{2}
. First, consider the case
p={\lambda}
. Then by (
4.13
), we take Hölder inequality to get

\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|\langle x\rangle^{{\lambda}-1}\,dx\,dt\leq\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{{\lambda}}\,dx\,dt+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|^{{\lambda}}\,dx\,dt.

Then we apply (
4.11
) to handle the last term on RHS which gives, for
\beta=1-\frac{(r_{1}-q)(d-2)}{d(m-1)+2q}
,

\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|\langle x\rangle^{{\lambda}-1}\,dx\,dt\leq\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{{\lambda}}\,dx\,dt+\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{{\lambda}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q,\infty}_{x,t}}^{\frac{q\beta}{r_{1}}}\,\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,t}}^{\frac{2}{r_{2}}}.

On the other hand, we consider the case
1<p<{\lambda}
in
J_{2}
from (
4.16
). Then we apply (
4.13
) and Young’s inequality with
\frac{p-1}{p}+\frac{1}{p}=1
to obtain

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq p\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|\langle x\rangle^{p-1}\,dx\,dt\leq\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+c(p)\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|^{p}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+c(p)\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|^{{\lambda}}\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{p}{{\lambda}}}\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{{\lambda}-p}{{\lambda}}}

\displaystyle\leq\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+c(p)T^{\frac{{\lambda}-p}{{\lambda}}}\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{\frac{{\lambda}-p}{{\lambda}}}\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|^{{\lambda}}\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{p}{{\lambda}}},

by taking another Hölder inequality with
\frac{p}{{\lambda}}+\frac{{\lambda}-p}{{\lambda}}=1
, and applying the mass conservation property.
By (
4.11
) and Young’s inequality with
\frac{p}{{\lambda}r_{2}}+\frac{{\lambda}r_{2}-p}{{\lambda}r_{2}}=1
, we have the following, for any
\varepsilon>0
,

J_{2}\leq\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x,t}}^{2}+c(\varepsilon,p)T^{\frac{r_{2}({\lambda}-p)}{{\lambda}r_{2}-p}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q,\infty}_{x,t}}^{\frac{qp\beta r_{2}}{r_{1}({\lambda}r_{2}-p)}}\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{\frac{p{\lambda}r_{2}}{{\lambda}r_{2}-p}}.

(4.18)

The combination of the above with (
4.16
) and (
4.17
) completes the proof.

Remark 4.4

A similar estimate as in above lemma holds for
L^{q}
-weak solutions that are narrowly continuous in time variable. For the sketch of proof, let
\eta:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
be a smooth cut-off function satisfying

\eta(x)=\ \begin{cases}1,&\text{ if }\ |x|\leq 1,\\
0,&\text{ if }\ |x|\geq 2.\end{cases}

Let
\rho
be a
L^{q}
-weak solution of (
1.1
) such that
\rho:[0,T]\mapsto{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
is narrowly continuous. For
\delta>0
, we define
\eta_{\delta}(x):=\eta(\delta x)
. As in Lemma
4.3
, by testing
\langle x\rangle^{p}\eta_{\delta}
to (
1.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\left(\langle x\rangle^{p}\eta_{\delta}(x)\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\left(\langle x\rangle^{p}\eta_{\delta}(x)\right)\,dx

(4.19)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\rho^{m}\Delta\left(\langle x\rangle^{p}\eta_{\delta}(x)\right)\right|\,dx\,dt}_{:=J_{3}}+\underbrace{\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\rho V\cdot\nabla\left(\langle x\rangle^{p}\eta_{\delta}(x)\right)\right|\,dx\,dt.}_{:=J_{4}}

We note that
\|\nabla\langle x\rangle^{p}\nabla\eta_{\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}},~\|\langle x\rangle^{p}\Delta\eta_{\delta}\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\lesssim\delta^{2-p}
. Hence

\displaystyle J_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\rho^{m}\Delta\langle x\rangle^{p}\right|\eta_{\delta}(x)\,dx\,dt+\delta^{2-p}\iint_{\{\frac{1}{\delta}\leq|x|\leq\frac{2}{\delta}\}\times[0,T]}\rho^{m}\,dx\,dt

(4.20)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\rho^{m}\Delta\langle x\rangle^{p}\right|\,dx\,dt\quad\mbox{as}~~\delta\rightarrow 0.

Similarly, we note
|\langle x\rangle^{p}\nabla\eta_{\delta}|\lesssim|\langle x\rangle^{p-1}|
and have

\displaystyle J_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\rho V\cdot\nabla\langle x\rangle^{p}\right|\eta_{\delta}(x)\,dx\,dt+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|\langle x\rangle^{p-1}\,dx\,dt

(4.21)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\rho V\cdot\nabla\langle x\rangle^{p}\right|\,dx\,dt+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho|V|\langle x\rangle^{p-1}\,dx\,dt,\quad\mbox{as}~~\delta\rightarrow 0.

Finally, we take
\delta\rightarrow 0
in (
4.19
). Then, we combine (
4.17
)–(
4.18
), (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) to have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\rho(\cdot,t)\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx+2\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt

(4.22)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt+c_{1}T+c_{2}T^{\beta_{1}\frac{{\lambda_{q}}-p}{{\lambda_{q}}}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q,\infty}_{x,t}}^{\beta\beta_{1}\frac{qp(q_{1}-{\lambda_{q}})}{q_{1}{\lambda_{q}}}}\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}}^{p\beta_{1}},

where
c_{1},\,c_{2},\,\beta
and
\beta_{1}
are same as in Lemma
4.3
.

4.2
Energy estimates

This subsection is composed with energy estimates for two different cases: first when
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx<\infty
and second when
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}<\infty
for
q>1
.

4.2.1
Energy estimates for case:
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx<\infty
.

Under additional assumption
\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, not merely
\rho_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we obtain a priori estimate categorized three cases depending on the range of
m
and the conditions on
V
.

Proposition 4.5

Let
m>1
,
1<p\leq{\lambda_{1}}:=1+\frac{1}{d(m-1)+1}
. Assume that
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
holds

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx<\infty.

(4.23)

Suppose that
\rho
is a regular solution of (
1.1
) with sufficiently fast decay at infinity in spatial variables.

(i)

Let
1<m\leq 2
. Assume that

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}2\leq q_{2}\leq\frac{m}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
2\leq q_{2}<\frac{m}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.24)

Then the following estimate holds

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho\left(\left|\log\rho\right|+\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\frac{2}{m}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.25)

where
C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx)
.

(ii)

Assume that

V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}\frac{2+d(m-1)}{1+d(m-1)}<\tilde{q}_{2}\leq\frac{m}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
\frac{2+d(m-1)}{1+d(m-1)}<\tilde{q}_{2}<\frac{m}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.26)

Then the estimate (
4.25
) holds where

C=C(\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx).

(iii)

Let
\nabla\cdot V=0
. Assume that

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}{\lambda_{1}}\leq q_{2}\leq\frac{{\lambda_{1}}m}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
{\lambda_{1}}\leq q_{2}<\frac{{\lambda_{1}}m}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.27)

Then the estimate (
4.25
) holds where

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx).

Proof.

First, we recall the
p
-entropy inequality (for example, see
[
27
,
16
]
)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\left|\log\rho\right|\,dx\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\log\rho\,dx+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx+c(d),

(4.28)

which gives

0\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\log\rho\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\left(\log\rho\right)_{-}\,dx\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\log\rho\,dx+d\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx+c(d).

(4.29)

By testing
\log\rho
to (
1.1
), we obtain

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\log\rho\,dx+\frac{4}{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\cdot\nabla\rho\,dx=:\mathcal{J}.

(4.30)

(i)
For
1<m<2
, we write RHS of (
4.30
) as

\mathcal{J}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\cdot\nabla\rho\,dx\leq\frac{2}{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|V\right|\rho^{1-\frac{m}{2}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|\,dx.

By Hölder inequality with
\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{q_{1}-2}{2q_{1}}=1
for
q_{1}>2
, we have

\mathcal{J}\leq c\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\rho^{\frac{2-m}{2}}\right\|_{L^{\frac{2q_{1}}{q_{1}-2}}_{x}}\leq c\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\rho\right\|^{\frac{2-m}{2}}_{L^{\frac{(2-m)q_{1}}{q_{1}-2}}_{x}}.

(4.31)

When
m=2
, the above inequality corresponds to

\mathcal{J}\leq c\|V\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}
.

For convinence, we denote

r_{1}:=\frac{(2-m)q_{1}}{q_{1}-2},\quad\text{and}\quad\theta:=\frac{1}{r_{1}}\left(1-\frac{(r_{1}-1)(d-2)}{2+d(m-1)}\right).

Then by (
3.2
) in Lemma
3.4
, we find that

\begin{cases}2<\frac{md}{1+(m-1)(d-1)}\leq q_{1}\leq\frac{2}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
2<q_{1}\leq\frac{2}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.32)

Moreover, the combination of (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.4
and (
4.31
) implies that

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}

\displaystyle\leq c\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\rho\right\|^{\frac{2-m}{2}}_{L^{\frac{(2-m)q_{1}}{q_{1}-2}}_{x}}\leq c\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}\left\|\rho\right\|^{\frac{\theta(2-m)}{2}}_{L^{1}_{x}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right\|^{1+\frac{(1-\theta)(2-m)}{m}}_{L^{2}_{x}}

(4.33)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{x}}+c(\varepsilon)\|V\|^{\frac{2m}{m-(1-\theta)(2-m)}}_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}},

by taking Young’s inequality. Since
V
is in
\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
, it follows that

\frac{2m}{m-(1-\theta)(2-m)}=\frac{q_{1}(2+d(m-1))}{q_{1}(1+d(m-1))-d}=q_{2}.

Moreover, (
4.32
) corresponds to
(
4.24
).

We combine (
4.30
) and (
4.33
), and then take integration in terms of
t
with
\varepsilon=\frac{2}{m}
to have

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\log\rho\,dx+\frac{2}{m}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx+c\|V\|^{q_{2}}_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}.

(4.34)

To (
4.34
), we apply (
4.29
) and add
p
-th moment estimate (
4.15
) with
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{m}
to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho\left|\log\rho\right|+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\frac{1}{m}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt

(4.35)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}),

where
(q_{1},q_{2})
given in (
4.24
) that satisfies (
4.2
) too.
To handle the integral of
p
-th moment on RHS of (
4.35
), we apply the Grönwall’s inequality that leads to

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho\left|\log\rho\right|+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\frac{1}{m}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}})\right].

Therefore, we complete to obtain (
4.25
) under (
4.24
).

(ii)
First we take the integration by parts and apply Hölder inequality for
\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{1}}+\frac{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}{\tilde{q}_{1}}=1
to have

\mathcal{J}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\cdot\nabla\rho\,dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\nabla\cdot V\,dx\leq\|\nabla V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{q}_{1}}}\|\rho\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{\tilde{q}_{1}}{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}}}.

(4.36)

For convenience, we denote

\tilde{r}_{1}:=\frac{\tilde{q}_{1}}{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\theta:=\frac{1}{\tilde{r}_{1}}\left(1-\frac{(\tilde{r}_{1}-1)(d-2)}{2+d(m-1)}\right).

Then Lemma
3.4
with
\tilde{r}_{1}
in the place for
r_{1}
implies that

\begin{cases}1<\frac{md}{2+d(m-1)}\leq\tilde{q}_{1}\leq\infty,&\text{ if }d>2,\\
1<\tilde{q}_{1}<\infty,&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.37)

Then the combination of (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.4
and (
4.36
) gives

\mathcal{J}\leq c\|\nabla V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{q}_{1}}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{\frac{m}{m-(1-\theta)}}\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{2}+c(\varepsilon)\|\nabla V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{q}_{1}}}^{\frac{m}{m-(1-\theta)}},

(4.38)

by taking Young’s inequality. Because of
V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}
, it gives

\frac{m}{m-(1-\theta)}=\frac{\tilde{q}_{1}(2+d(m-1))}{\tilde{q}_{1}(2+d(m-1))-2d}=\tilde{q}_{2}.

Moreover, (
4.37
) corresponds to (
4.26
).

Now we combine (
4.30
) and (
4.38
), and then take integration in terms of
t
with
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{m}
to have

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\log\rho\,dx+\frac{3}{m}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx+c\|V\|^{\tilde{q}_{2}}_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}.

(4.39)

To (
4.39
), we apply (
4.29
) and add
p
-th moment estimate (
4.15
) with
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{m}
to obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho\left|\log\rho\right|+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\frac{2}{m}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}),

under (
4.26
) and
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
holding (
4.2
) for
q=1
.
By Remark
1.2

(ii)
, we see that (
4.26
) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho\left|\log\rho\right|+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\frac{2}{m}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+C(\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}).

Then we complete the proof by taking Grönwall’s inequality to handle the integral of
p
-th moment as in (
4.35
).

(iii)
Because of (
4.36
) and
\nabla\cdot V=0
, we observe that RHS of (
4.30
) vanishes. Therefore, we obtain the following by taking integral to (
4.30
) with repect to
t
:

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\log\rho\,dx+\frac{4}{m}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx.

(4.40)

For
V
satisfying (
4.2
) which is (
4.27
) when
q=1
, we add (
4.15
) to (
4.40
). Then as above, we apply (
4.29
) and Grönwall’s inequality to complete the proof. We note that (
4.27
) comes directly from (
4.2
).

4.2.2
Energy estimates for case:
\rho_{0}\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ q>1
.

In the following proposition, there are three cases for
m,q>1
. First case is when
q-m+1\geq 0
holds. The second case cares all
m,q>1
by prescribing conditions on
\nabla V
, and the last case is when
\nabla\cdot V=0
.

Proposition 4.6

Let
m,q>1
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Suppose that
\rho
is a regular solution of (
1.1
) with sufficiently fast decay at infinity in spatial variables.

(i)

Let
q\geq m-1
. Assume that

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}2\leq q_{2}\leq\frac{q+m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
2\leq q_{2}<\frac{q+m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.41)

Then the following estimate holds:

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}(\cdot,t)\,dx+\frac{2qm(q-1)}{(q+m-1)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.42)

where
C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
.

(ii)

Assume that

V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}1\leq\tilde{q}_{2}\leq\frac{q+m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
1\leq\tilde{q}_{2}<\frac{q+m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.43)

Then (
4.42
) holds with

C=C(\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}},\,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
.

(iii)

Assume that
V\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
and
\nabla\cdot V=0
. Then the following estimate holds

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}(\cdot,t)\,dx+\frac{qm(q-1)}{(q+m-1)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}^{q}\,dx.

(4.44)

Proof.

Let
q>1
. By testing
\varphi=q\rho^{q-1}
to (
1.1
), we have

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}\,dx+\frac{4mq(q-1)}{(q+m-1)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx=q\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\rho\nabla\rho^{q-1}\,dx=:\mathcal{J}.

(4.45)

(i)
In case
q\geq m-1
, we observe that

\mathcal{J}=q\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\rho\nabla\rho^{q-1}\,dx=\frac{2(q-1)}{q+m-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\rho^{\frac{q-m+1}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\,dx.

(4.46)

First, let
q>m-1
. Then by Hölder inequality with
\frac{1}{q_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{q_{1}-2}{2q_{1}}=1
for
q_{1}>2
, we have

\mathcal{J}\leq c\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\|\rho\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{q_{1}(q-m+1)}{q_{1}-2}}}^{\frac{q-m+1}{2}}.

Furthermore, when
q=m-1
, we have

\mathcal{J}=c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\,dx\leq c\|V\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+c(\varepsilon)\|V\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}.

For simplicity, let

r_{1}:=\frac{q_{1}(q-m+1)}{q_{1}-2},\quad\text{and}\quad\theta:=\frac{q}{r_{1}}\left(1-\frac{(r_{1}-q)(d-2)}{2q+d(m-1)}\right).

Then by (
3.2
) in Lemma
3.4
, we see that

\begin{cases}2<\frac{d(q+m-1)}{q+(d-1)(m-1)}\leq q_{1}\leq\frac{2q}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
2<q_{1}\leq\frac{2q}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.47)

Then by (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.4
, we deduce

\mathcal{J}\leq c\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{1+\frac{(1-\theta)(q-m+1)}{q+m-1}}\|\rho\|_{L_{x}^{q}}^{\frac{\theta(q-m+1)}{2}}\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+c(\varepsilon)\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}^{q_{2}}\|\rho\|_{L_{x}^{q}}^{q_{2}\frac{\theta(q-m+1)}{2}},

(4.48)

by taking Young’s inequality. Due to
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
, it gives

\frac{2(q+m-1)}{(q+m-1)-(1-\theta)(q-m+1)}=\frac{q_{1}(2+q_{m,d})}{q_{1}(1+q_{m,d})-d}=q_{2}.

The conditions (
4.47
) corrresponds to (
4.41
).

Now the combination of (
4.45
) and (
4.48
) with
\varepsilon=\frac{4mq(q-1)}{(q+m-1)^{2}}
yields that

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}\,dx\leq c\|V\|_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}}^{q_{2}}\|\rho\|_{L_{x}^{q}}^{q_{2}\frac{\theta(q-m+1)}{2}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}\,dx\right)^{1-\sigma}\leq c\|V\|^{q_{2}}_{L^{q_{1}}_{x}},

because
\sigma:=\frac{\theta q_{2}(q-m+1)}{2q}=\frac{\theta q_{2}(q_{1}-2)}{2qq_{1}}<1
. By taking the integration in terms of
t
, and taking power by
\frac{1}{1-\sigma}
, we obtain the following :

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}(t)\,dx\leq c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}^{q}\,dx+c\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{c}.

(4.49)

We combine (
4.45
) and (
4.48
) with
\varepsilon=\frac{2mq(q-1)}{(q+m-q)^{2}}
and take the integration in terms of
t
, which yields

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}\,dx

\displaystyle+\frac{2mq(q-1)}{(q+m-1)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt

(4.50)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}^{q}\,dx+c\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\rho(\cdot,t)\|_{L_{x}^{q}}^{q_{2}\frac{\theta(q-m+1)}{2}}.

Therefore, we obtain (
4.42
) by substituting (
4.49
) into (
4.50
).

(ii)
Here, we take the integration by parts that gives:

\mathcal{J}=q\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\rho\nabla\rho^{q-1}\,dx=(q-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}V\nabla\rho^{q}\,dx=-(q-1)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla V\rho^{q}\,dx.

(4.51)

By taking Hölder inequality with
\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{1}}+\frac{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}{\tilde{q}_{1}}=1
for
\tilde{q}_{1}>1
, we have

\mathcal{J}\leq c\|\nabla V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{q}_{1}}}\|\rho\|_{L^{\frac{q\tilde{q}_{1}}{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}}_{x}}^{q}.

For simplicity, let

\tilde{r}_{1}:=\frac{q\tilde{q}_{1}}{\tilde{q}_{1}-1}\quad\text{ and }\quad\theta:=\frac{q}{\tilde{r}_{1}}\left(1-\frac{(\tilde{r}_{1}-q)(d-2)}{2q+d(m-1)}\right).

Then by (
3.2
) in Lemma
3.4
for
\tilde{r}_{1}
instead of
r_{1}
, it gives

\begin{cases}1<\frac{d+q_{m,d}}{2+q_{m,d}}\leq\tilde{q}_{1}\leq\infty,&\text{ if }d>2,\\
1<\tilde{q}_{1}\leq\infty,&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.52)

Then by (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) in Lemma
3.4
, we deduce

\mathcal{J}\leq c\|\nabla V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{q}_{1}}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q\theta}\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{\frac{2q(1-\theta)}{q+m-1}}\leq\varepsilon\left\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+c(\varepsilon)\|\nabla V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{q}_{1}}}^{\tilde{q}_{2}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q\theta\tilde{q}_{2}},

(4.53)

by taking Young’s inequality. Because of
V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}
, it gives

\frac{q+m-1}{(q+m-1)-(1-\theta)q}=\frac{\tilde{q}_{1}(2+q_{m,d})}{\tilde{q}_{1}(2+q_{m,d})-d}=\tilde{q}_{2}.

Also, we observe that (
4.52
) corresponds to (
4.43
).

Now we combine (
4.45
) and (
4.53
) with
\varepsilon=\frac{4mq(q-1)}{(q-m+1)^{2}}
which yields

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}(\cdot,t)\,dx\leq c\|\nabla V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{q}_{1}}}^{\tilde{q}_{2}}\|\rho\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q\theta\tilde{q}_{2}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\frac{d}{dt}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}\,dx\right)^{1-\sigma}\leq c\|\nabla V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{q}_{1}}}^{\tilde{q}_{2}},

(4.54)

because
\sigma:=\theta\tilde{q}_{2}<1
.
By taking the integration in terms of
t
, and taking power by
\frac{1}{1-\sigma}
, we obtain the following :

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}(t)\,dx\leq c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}^{q}\,dx+c\|V\|_{L_{x}^{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}}}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}.

(4.55)

To obtain (
4.42
), we add (
4.45
) and (
4.53
) with
\varepsilon=\frac{2mq(q-1)}{(q-m+1)^{2}}
. We complete the proof by taking integration in terms of
t
and applying (
4.54
).

(iii)
It is trivial that
\mathcal{J}
is vanishing when (
4.51
) is combined with
\nabla\cdot V=0
. Therefore, (
4.44
) follows directly by taking integration of (
4.45
) in terms of
t
and applying (
4.55
).

Under the same hyphthesis in Proposition
4.6
, we prove the following proposition.

Proposition 4.7

Let
m,q>1
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Suppose that
\rho
is a regular solution of (
1.1
) with sufficiently fast decay at infinity in spatial variables.

(i)

Let
q\geq m-1
and assume that
V
satisfies (
4.41
).
Then, for any
r>1
and
m-1\leq r\leq q
, it holds

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{r}(\cdot,t)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{r+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.56)

where
C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
.

(ii)

If
V
satisfies (
4.43
), then the estimate (
4.56
) holds with

C=C(\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}},\,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
for any
1<r\leq q
.

(iii)

If
V\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
and
\nabla\cdot V=0
, then the estimate (
4.56
) holds with

C=C(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
for any
1<r\leq q
.

Remark 4.8

Assuming further that
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
p>1
and
V
satisfies (
4.2
), then the estimate (
4.56
) holds for
(i)

\max\{1,m-1\}\leq r\leq q
, and
(ii)
,
(iii)

1\leq r\leq q
with a constant
C
depending also on

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\cdot\delta_{\{r=1\}}
.

Proof.
We note a couple of remarks.
Firstly, note that
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\leq C(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}\cap L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})})
for any
r\in(1,q)
by the interpolation inequality because
\rho_{0}\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. Secondly,
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
implies that
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,r}^{(q_{1},q_{2}^{r})}
for any
r\in[1,q)
by (
1.9
) in Remark
1.2

(iii)
.

Then the estimate (
4.56
) follows by similar arguments as in Proposition
4.6
for
r
in the place of
q
. Then
C
depends on
\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,r}^{(q_{1},q_{2}^{r})}}
and
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}
which are replaced by
\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}
and
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}
because of earlier remarks.

Especially, in case
(i)
, we carry similar analysis of (
4.46
) with
r
instead of
q
if
r\geq m-1
. In case
(ii)
, note that
V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}
implies the membership in
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,r}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2}^{r})}
for any
r\in[1,q]
by Remark
1.2
.
In case
(iii)
, the estimate (
4.56
) holds for any
r\in(1,q]
with
C=C(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
using the divergence-free condition.
This completes the proof.

4.3
Estimates of speed and
p
-th moment

Here we obtain speed and
p
-th moment estimates by combining the auxiliary and energy estimates in earlier subsections.
First result is when
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx<\infty
for
1<p\leq{\lambda_{1}}
.

Proposition 4.9

Let
m>1
and
1<p\leq{\lambda_{1}}:=1+\frac{1}{d(m-1)+1}
. Under the same hypothesis in Proposition
4.5
, the followings hold true.

(i)

Let
1<m\leq 2
.
Assume that
V
satisfies (
4.24
).
Then the following estimate holds that

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho\left(\left|\log\rho\right|+\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left(\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{p}+|V|^{p}\right)\rho\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.57)

where
C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx)
.

(ii)

Assume that
V
satisfies (
4.26
).
Then the estimate (
4.57
) holds with

C=C(\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx).

(iii)

Let
\nabla\cdot V=0
. Assume that
V
satisfies (
4.27
).
Then the estimate (
4.57
) holds with

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx).

Proof.

To obtain (
4.57
), for
q=1
, we add (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) to (
4.25
) with
2\epsilon=\frac{1}{m}
.

Now when
\rho_{0}\in L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
,
q>1
, we compute the following estimates assuming additionally
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx<\infty
.

Proposition 4.10

Let
m,q>1
and
1<p\leq{\lambda_{q}}:=\min\left\{2,1+\frac{d(q-1)+q}{d(m-1)+q}\right\}
. Suppose that
\rho_{0}\in L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
and the same hypothesis in Proposition
4.6
hold.

(i)

Let
q\geq m-1
. Assume that

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}q_{1}\leq\frac{2m}{m-1},\ 2\leq q_{2}\leq\frac{q+m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{1<q\leq m\}}+\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{q>m\}},&\text{if }d>2,\\
q_{1}\leq\frac{2m}{m-1},\ 2\leq q_{2}<\frac{q+m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{1<q\leq m\}}+\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{q>m\}},&\text{if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.58)

Then the following estimate holds

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho^{q}+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left(\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{p}+|V|^{p}\right)\rho\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.59)

where
C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}^{q}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx)
.

(ii)

Assume that

V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}\frac{2+q_{m,d}}{1+q_{m,d}}<\tilde{q}_{2}\leq\frac{q+m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{1<q\leq m\}}+\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{q>m\}},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
\frac{2+q_{m,2}}{1+q_{m,2}}<\tilde{q}_{2}<\frac{q+m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{1<q\leq m\}}+\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{q>m\}},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.60)

Then (
4.59
) holds with

C=C(\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}^{q}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx).

(iii)

Let
\nabla\cdot V=0
. Assume that

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}{\lambda_{q}}\leq q_{2}\leq\frac{{\lambda_{q}}(q+m-1)}{q+m-2},&\text{ if }d>2,\ 1<q\leq m,\\
q_{1}\leq\frac{2m}{m-1},\ 2\leq q_{2}\leq\frac{2m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\ q>m,\\
{\lambda_{q}}\leq q_{2}<\frac{{\lambda_{q}}(q+m-1)}{q+m-2},&\text{ if }d=2,\ 1<q\leq m,\\
q_{1}\leq\frac{2m}{m-1},\ 2\leq q_{2}<\frac{2m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2,\ q>m.\end{cases}

(4.61)

Then (
4.59
) holds with

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}^{q}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx).

Proof.
Here we carry computations for each cases.

(i)
First, let
m-1\leq q\leq m
. We add (
4.42
) and (
4.15
) with
\varepsilon=\frac{mq(q-1)}{(m+q-1)^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho^{q}+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\frac{mq(q-1)}{(m+q-1)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}^{q}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+C(\|V\|_{S_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}).

To handle the
p
-th moment term on RHS, we apply Grönwall’s inequality as in the proof of Proposition
4.5
:

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho^{q}+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\frac{mq(q-1)}{(m+q-1)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.62)

where
C=C(\|V\|_{S_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}^{q}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx)
.
Then we obtain (
4.59
) by adding (
4.3
) and (
4.4
) with
2\varepsilon=\frac{mq(q-1)}{(m+q-1)^{2}}
to (
4.62
). We note that (
4.58
) is the intersected region of (
4.2
) and (
4.41
).

Now let
q>m
, so
{\lambda_{q}}=2
. Then
V
satisfying (
4.58
) also holds (
4.41
). Therefore, we have an estimate as in (
4.42
):

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}).

(4.63)

Furthermore, it is true that
\rho_{0}\in L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
(in fact,
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq c(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
) because
\rho_{0}\in L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
and assumption
q>m
. Then the corresponding energy estimate under
V
satisfying (
4.58
) is

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{m}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{2m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,m}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}),

(4.64)

in which
q
in (
4.63
) is replaced by
m
under (
4.58
). Then for
q_{1}
holding (
4.58
), the combination of (
4.63
) and (
4.64
) provides

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{2m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}),

because of (
1.9
) in Remark
1.2
(iii).

Now we recall the speed and
p
-th moment estimates (
4.2
), (
4.2
), and (
4.15
) at
q=m
that, for
1<p\leq 2
,

\iint_{Q_{T}}\left(\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{p}+|V|^{p}\right)\rho\,dx\,dt\leq\varepsilon\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{2m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt+C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,m}^{(q_{1},\bar{q}_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}),

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{2m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt+C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,m}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}),

for any
\varepsilon>0
where
V
satisfies (
4.58
) which also holds (
4.2
). Then the combination with (
4.63
) using (
1.9
) in Remark
1.2
(iii)
implies the follwoing estimate

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho^{q}+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left(\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{p}+|V|^{p}\right)\rho\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\,\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\,dt+C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}).

By the Grönwall’s inequality, we complete the proof to obtain (
4.59
) under (
4.58
).

(ii)
By following similar computations as in above case, we can obtain also (
4.59
). The condition on
V
in (
4.60
) follows as the intersected region of (
4.2
). (
4.43
), and (
1.8
). Because
{\lambda_{q}}
behaves differently depending on whether
1<q\leq m
or
q>m
, (
4.2
) and (
4.43
) determines the upper bound of
\tilde{q}_{2}
in (
4.58
).
By considering (
1.8
) which affects the lower bound of
q_{2}
in (
4.58
), hence
C
depends on
\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}
.

(iii)
To yield (
4.59
), we add (
4.15
), (
4.3
), and (
4.4
) to (
4.44
) by following above computations. Because (
4.44
) is independent of
V
, only (
4.2
) is required in this case, that is, (
4.61
).

Now let us switch a gear to obtain speed and moment estimates based on embedding arguments in time direction assuming
\rho_{0}\in L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. The embedding argument is possible beacuse of the finite time interval.

Proposition 4.11

(Embedding)
Assume the same hypothesis as in Proposition
4.10
.

(i)

Let
q>m
. Assume that

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}\frac{2m-1}{m-1}<q_{2}\leq\frac{q+m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\leq q_{2}<\frac{q+m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.65)

For given
(q_{1},q_{2})
in (
4.65
), there exists a constant
q_{2}^{\ast}=q_{2}^{\ast}(q_{1})\in[2,q_{2})
such that
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(q_{1},q_{2}^{\ast})}
satisfies (
4.58
) and the following estimate, for
1<p\leq{\lambda_{q^{\ast}}}:=\min\left\{2,1+\frac{d(q^{\ast}-1)+q^{\ast}}{d(m-1)+q^{\ast}}\right\}
,

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho^{q^{\ast}}+\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left(\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{p}+|V|^{p}\right)\rho\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.66)

where

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(q_{1},q^{\ast}_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\rho_{0}^{q^{\ast}}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx)
.

(ii)

Let
q>m
. Assume that

V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}\frac{2m-1}{m-1}<\tilde{q}_{2}\leq\frac{q+m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\leq\tilde{q}_{2}<\frac{q+m-1}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.67)

For given
(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})
in (
4.67
), there exists a constant
\tilde{q}_{2}^{\ast}=\tilde{q}_{2}^{\ast}(\tilde{q}_{1})\in(\frac{2+q^{\ast}_{m,d}}{1+q^{\ast}_{m,d}},\tilde{q}_{2})
such that
V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2}^{\ast})}
satisfies (
4.60
) and
the estimate (
4.66
) holds for
1<p\leq{\lambda_{q^{\ast}}}
with
C=C(\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,\tilde{q}^{\ast}}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}^{\ast}_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\rho_{0}^{q^{\ast}}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx)
.

(iii)

Let
\nabla\cdot V=0
. Assume that

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})\quad\text{for}\quad\begin{cases}\frac{2+q_{m,d}}{1+q_{m,d}}<q_{2}\leq\frac{{\lambda_{1}}m}{m-1},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
\frac{2+q_{m,2}}{1+q_{m,2}}<q_{2}<\frac{{\lambda_{1}}m}{m-1},&\text{ if }d=2.\end{cases}

(4.68)

For given
(q_{1},q_{2})
in (
4.68
), there exists a constant
q_{2}^{\ast}=q_{2}^{\ast}(q_{1})\in[2,q_{2}]
such that
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(q_{1},q_{2}^{\ast})}
satisfies (
4.61
) and the estimate (
4.66
) holds for
1<p\leq{\lambda_{q^{\ast}}}
with

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(q_{1},q^{\ast}_{2})}},\,\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\rho_{0}^{q^{\ast}}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx)
.

Remark 4.12

To capture the idea of searching
q^{\ast}_{2}
and
q^{\ast}
in above proposition, see Figure 4-
(e)
, 5-
(e)
, 7-
(e)
, 8-
(e)
, and 9-
(e)
in Appendix
B
. For instance, in case
(i)
, for
\frac{d(2m-1)}{1+d(m-1)}<q_{1}<d(2m-1)
, one can choose

q_{2}^{\ast}=\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad q^{\ast}=\frac{md(m-1)}{d(2m-1)/q_{1}-1},

where
(q_{1},q_{2}^{\ast})
lies on
\mathcal{S}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(q_{1},q_{2}^{\ast})}
.
Moreover, in case
(ii)
, for
\frac{d(2m-1)}{2m+d(m-1)}<\tilde{q}_{1}<\frac{d(2m-1)}{2m}
, one may choose

\tilde{q}_{2}^{\ast}=\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad q^{\ast}=\frac{md(m-1)}{d(2m-1)/\tilde{q}_{1}-2m},

so that
(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}^{\ast}_{2})
lies on
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}^{\ast}_{2})}
.
In case
(iii)
, we note that, for
q_{1}<\frac{md}{1+d(m-1)}
(corresponding to
q_{2}>\frac{{\lambda_{1}}m}{m-1}
), there is no
p
-th moment and speed estimate although an energy inequality in (
4.44
) is possible to obtain.

Proof.

(i)
For any
(q_{1},q_{2})
in (
4.65
), one can find
q_{2}^{\ast}\in[2,q_{2})
satisfying

\begin{cases}2\leq q_{2}^{\ast}\leq\frac{q^{\ast}+m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{1<q^{\ast}\leq m\}}+\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{q^{\ast}>m\}},&\text{ if }d>2,\\
2\leq q_{2}^{\ast}<\frac{q^{\ast}+m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{1<q^{\ast}\leq m\}}+\frac{2m-1}{m-1}\,\delta_{\{q^{\ast}>m\}},&\text{ if }d=2,\end{cases}

(4.69)

which also determines
q^{\ast}\in(1,q)
such that
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(q_{1},q_{2}^{\ast})}
.
Because
q^{\ast}\in(1,q)
, the interpolation provides that
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q^{\ast}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq c(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
which means we have (
4.42
) for
q^{\ast}
in the place of
q
. Also because (
4.69
) is same with (
4.58
) for
q^{\ast}
, therefore, we are able to deduce (
4.66
) in terms of
q^{\ast}
, which completes the proof.

(ii)
Similar as above, for any
(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})
satisfying (
4.67
), one can find
\tilde{q}_{2}^{\ast}<\tilde{q}_{2}
and
q^{\ast}\in(1,q)
satisfying (
4.67
) where
q^{\ast}_{2}
and
q^{\ast}
are replaced for
q_{2}
and
q
. Then we have the estimate in Proposition
4.10

(ii)
with
q^{\ast}
and
{\lambda_{q^{\ast}}}
instead of
q
and
{\lambda_{q}}
.

(iii)
In this case, we first note that (
4.68
) contains the region by (
4.61
). Therefore, if
(q_{1},q_{2})
satisfies (
4.61
), then there is nothing to prove but apply results in Proposition
4.10

(iii)
directly.
Now let
(q_{1},q_{2})
holds (
4.68
) but (
4.61
), then we apply the similar arguments as above. One may choose
q_{2}^{\ast}<q_{2}
and
q^{\ast}\in(1,q)
satisfying (
4.61
) with
q^{\ast}_{2}
and
q^{\ast}
in the places of
q_{2}
and
q
. Then the estimate as in Proposition
4.10

(iii)
with
q^{\ast}
and
{\lambda_{q^{\ast}}}
instead of
q
and
{\lambda_{q}}
completes the proof.

4.4
Estimates of temporal and spatial derivatives.

Two main propositions in this subsection, we estimate temporal and spatial derivatives of
\rho
using the integrability of
\rho
from energy estimates and PDE (
1.1
).
Those estimates will be crucially used to show the strong convergence of nonlinear diffusion of approximated solutions.

Before we introduce the first proposition, we specify the integrability of
\rho
and
V\rho
based on energy estimate when
q=1
and parabolic embedding lemma.

Remark 4.13

From (
4.25
) in Proposition
4.5
, we observe that

\rho\in L^{\frac{md+2}{d}}_{x,t}
by Lemma
3.2
for
q=1
, that is,

\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho^{\frac{md+2}{d}}\,dxdt\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho(\cdot,t)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.70)

where the constant
C
depends on
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx
and either
\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}
or
\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}
.
Then it holds

\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|V\rho\right|^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}\,dx\,dt\leq\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}\|\rho\|_{L^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{x,t}}^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}},

(4.71)

for

\frac{1}{r_{1}}=\frac{md+1}{md+2}-\frac{1}{q_{1}}
and
\frac{1}{r_{2}}=\frac{md+1}{md+2}-\frac{1}{q_{2}}
.
For the pair
(q_{1},q_{2})
satisfying one of assumptions on
V
in Proposition
4.5
, the pair
(r_{1},r_{2})
satisfies (
3.2
) in Lemma
3.4
for
q=1
and the norm
\|\rho\|_{L^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{x,t}}
is bounded by the same
C
by (
3.3
).

In the following proposition, we control the temporal and spatial derivatives of
\rho
for
q=1
.

Proposition 4.14

Let
1<m\leq 2
. Assume that
(a)

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
satisfying (
4.24
), or
(b)

V\in\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}
satisfying (
4.26
), or
(c)
a divergence-free
V
satisfying (
4.27
).
Suppose that
\rho
is a regular solution of (
1.1
) with sufficiently fast decay at infity in spatial variables with
\rho_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
for
1<p\leq{\lambda_{1}}
holding (
4.23
).
Then it holds that

\left\|\rho_{t}\right\|_{W_{x}^{-1,\frac{md+2}{md+1}}L_{t}^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}}+\left\|\nabla\rho\right\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{md+2}{d+1}}}<C\left(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\left(1+\log\rho_{0}+\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx\right),

(4.72)

where
C
depends on
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\left(1+\log\rho_{0}+\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx
and either
\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}
or
\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}
.

Remark 4.15

Moreover, the following is straightforward by applying Lemma
3.1
to (
4.72
) with
p=\left(\frac{md+2}{d+1}\right)^{\ast}
,

\rho\in L^{p,\frac{md+2}{d+1}}_{x,t}\quad\text{for}\quad p=\frac{d(md+2)}{d(d-m)+(d-2)}.

The estimate (
4.72
) is essential to show the strong convergence of approximated solutions by the compactness arguments via Aubin-Lions Lemma (see Section
6
).

Proof.

Thanks to (
4.70
), we observe that
\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\in L^{2}_{x,t}
and
\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\in L^{\frac{2(md+2)}{md}}_{x,t}
.
Hence we have

\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\in L^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}_{x,t}\quad\text{and}\quad V\rho\in L^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}_{x,t}

(4.73)

because of
\frac{md+1}{md+2}=\frac{md}{2(md+2)}+\frac{1}{2}
and (
4.71
).
By rewriting PDE (
1.1
) in the form of
\rho_{t}=\nabla\cdot\left(2\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}-V\rho\right)
, it follows via (
4.73
) that
\rho_{t}\in{W^{-1,\frac{md+2}{md+1}}L_{t}^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}}
and
\rho_{t}
satisfies (
4.72
).

When
m=2
, the it is clear that
\nabla\rho\in L^{2}_{x,t}
.
Because
\rho^{\frac{2-m}{2}}\in L^{\frac{2(md+2)}{d(2-m)}}_{x,t}
for
1<m<2
, we observe that

\nabla\rho=\frac{2}{m}\rho^{\frac{2-m}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\in L^{\frac{md+2}{d+1}}_{x,t},\quad\text{for}\quad\frac{d+1}{md+2}=\frac{d(2-m)}{2(md+2)}+\frac{1}{2},

and
\nabla\rho
satisfies (
4.72
) which completes the proof.

Now with the initial data in
L^{q}_{x}
for
q>1
, we search further properties of the temporal and spatial derivatives of
\rho^{q}
and
\rho^{q}
itself. First, we recall a priori estimates in Proposition
4.6
and remark the integrability of
\rho
and
V\rho^{q}
.

Remark 4.16

From the estimate (
4.42
) in Proposition
4.6
, it holds that
\rho\in L^{(q+m-1)+\frac{2q}{d}}_{x,t}
by Lemma
3.2
, that is,

\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho^{(q+m-1)+\frac{2q}{d}}\,dxdt\leq\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho^{q}(\cdot,t)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(4.74)

where the constant
C
is depending on
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}
and either
\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}
or
\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}
.

We carry the following lemma before Proposition
4.19
.

Lemma 4.17

Let
m>1
and
q\geq 1
. Suppose that
V
satisfies (
2.17
).
Then, it holds

V\rho^{q}\in L_{x,t}^{r}\quad\text{for}\quad r=\frac{d(m+q-1)+2q}{d(m+q-1)+q}.

Futhermore, the followin estimate holds

\left\|V\rho^{q}\right\|_{L_{x,t}^{r}}\leq\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}\|\rho\|_{L^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{x,t}}^{q}

(4.75)

where the pair
(r_{1},r_{2})=(\frac{rqq_{1}}{q_{1}-r},\frac{rqq_{2}}{q_{2}-r})
satisfies (
3.2
) in Lemma
3.4
.

Remark 4.18

When
q=1
, we easily observe that the estimate (
4.75
) coincides with the estimate (
4.71
). The estimate (
4.75
) is essential to prove compactness arguments in Proposition
4.19
.

Proof.

By taking the Hölder inequalities, we obtain the estimate (
4.75
) for
\frac{q}{r_{1}}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q_{1}}
and
\frac{q}{r_{2}}=\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q_{2}}
. For the pair
(r_{1},r_{2})
satisfying (
3.2
) and the pair
(q_{1},q_{2})
satisfying
\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
, it provides that

\frac{d+2+q_{m,d}}{r}=q\left(\frac{d}{r_{1}}+\frac{2+q_{m,d}}{r_{2}}\right)+\left(\frac{d}{q_{1}}+\frac{2+q_{m,d}}{q_{2}}\right)=d+1+q_{m,d}\ \Longrightarrow\ r=\frac{d(m+q-1)+2q}{d(m+q-1)+q}.

Let
d>2
. Then the range of
q\leq r_{1}\leq\frac{d(m+q-1)}{d-2}
in (
3.2
) yields the following

\frac{1}{r}-1\leq\frac{1}{q_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{r}-\frac{q(d-2)}{d(q+m-1)}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad 0\leq\frac{1}{q_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{q_{1}^{m}}:=\frac{2+q_{m,d}}{d+q_{m,d}}-\frac{1}{d+2+q_{m,d}}<1.

Moreover, the range of
q+m-1\leq r_{2}\leq\infty
yields

\frac{1}{r}-\frac{q}{q+m-1}\leq\frac{1}{q_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{r}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\frac{d(m+q-1)+q}{d(m+q-1)+2q}-\frac{q}{q+m-1}\leq\frac{1}{q_{2}}\leq\frac{1+q_{m,d}}{2+q_{m,d}},

where the lower bound becomes zero if
q=q^{\ast}
defined in (
2.16
) and becomes negative if
q>q^{\ast}
(this allows
q_{2}=\infty
for any
q\geq q^{\ast}
). In case
d=2
, the range of
q+m-1<r_{2}\leq\infty
in (
3.2
) corresponds to

\frac{1}{r}-\frac{q}{q+m-1}<\frac{1}{q_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{r}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\frac{d(m+q-1)+q}{d(m+q-1)+2q}-\frac{q}{q+m-1}<\frac{1}{q_{2}}\leq\frac{1+q_{m,d}}{2+q_{m,d}},

that allows
q_{2}=\infty
if
q>q^{\ast}
. Hence we derive restrictions on
V
in (
2.17
) which completes the proof.

Now, we estimate temporal and spatial derivatives of
\rho^{q}
for
q>1
when
\rho_{0}\in L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
.

Proposition 4.19

Let
m,q>1
. Assume that
(a)

V
satisfying (
4.41
), or
(b)

V
satisfying (
4.43
), or
(c)
a divergence-free
V\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
satisfying (
2.17
).
Suppose that
\rho
is a regular solution of (
1.1
) with sufficiently fast decay at infity in spatial variables with
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

(i)

Let
q\geq m-1
. Then, it holds that

\left\|\partial_{t}\rho^{q}\right\|_{W_{x}^{-1,\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{d(q+m-1)+q}}L_{t}^{1}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{q}\right\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{q(d+1)}}}<C,

(4.76)

where the constant
C
depends on
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}
and either
\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}
or
\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}
.

(ii)

In cases
(b)
or
(c)
holds, let
m>2
and
\frac{m}{2}<q<m-1
. Then it holds that

\left\|\partial_{t}\rho^{q}\right\|_{W_{x}^{-1,\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{d(q+m-1)+q}}L_{t}^{1}}+\left\|\nabla\rho^{q}\right\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{2d(q+m-1)+4q}{d(3q-r)+2q}}}<C,

(4.77)

for any
r\in(1,q]
and where
C
depends on
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}
and either
\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}
or
\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}
.

Remark 4.20

(i)

When
1<m\leq 2
, the above works for all
q>1
. When
m>2
, it works for only
q\geq m-1
, in general. However, in cases
V
satisfying either
(b)
or
(c)
, we are able to weaken the restriction, that is,
q>\frac{m}{2}
with the aid of the entropy estimation. We note
q>\frac{m}{2}
is meaningful only for
m>2
.

(ii)

In case
(b)
or
(c)
holds, then we are able to extend the range of
r\in[1,q]
under further assumptions on
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
for
p>1
and
V
satisfying (
4.2
). It is because we need moment estimate when
r=1
(see Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
4.5
). Then the estimate (
4.77
) holds for
\frac{m}{2}\leq q\leq m-1
.

(iii)

By applying Sobolev embedding in Lemma
3.1
to (
4.76
) and (
4.77
), respectively, the followings hold

\rho^{q}\in L^{p,\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{q(d+1)}}_{x,t}\quad\text{for}\quad p=\frac{d^{2}(q+m+1)+2dq}{q(d^{2}-2)-d(m-1)},

(4.78)

\rho^{q}\in L^{p,\frac{2d(q+m-1)+4q}{d(3q-r)+2q}}_{x,t}\quad\text{for}\quad p=\frac{2d^{2}(q+m-1)+4dq}{d^{2}(3q-r)-2d(m-1)-4q}.

As we mentioned earlier in Remark
4.15
, the estimates (
4.76
) and (
4.77
) are essential to show the strong convergence of approximated solutions by the compactness arguments via Aubin-Lions Lemma (see Section
6
).

Proof.

(i)
Let
q\geq m-1
. Because of
\rho^{\frac{q-m+1}{2}}\in L_{x,t}^{\frac{2(d(q+m-1)+2q)}{d(q-m+1)}}
and
\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\in L^{2}_{x,t}
in Remark
4.16
, we obtain

\nabla\rho^{q}=\frac{2q}{q+m-1}\rho^{\frac{q-m+1}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\in L_{x,t}^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{q(d+1)}},

(4.79)

via
\frac{q(d+1)}{d(q+m-1)+2q}=\frac{d(q-m+1)}{2d(q+m-1)+4q}+\frac{1}{2}
which satifes (
4.76
).

In case
(a)
holds, we rewrite the PDE (
1.1
) as the following form:

\left(\rho^{q}\right)_{t}=\nabla\cdot\left(c_{0}\rho^{\frac{q+m+1}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}-qV\rho^{q}\right)-c_{1}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}+(q-1)V\nabla\rho^{q},

(4.80)

where
c_{0}=\frac{2mq}{q+m-1}
and
c_{1}=\frac{4mq(q-1)}{(q+m-1)^{2}}
.
To handle the cases of
(b)
or
(c)
, we alternatively rewrite the equation as

\left(\rho^{q}\right)_{t}=\nabla\cdot\left(c_{0}\rho^{\frac{q+m+1}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}-V\rho^{q}\right)-c_{1}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}-(q-1)\rho^{q}\,\nabla\cdot V.

(4.81)

Because
\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\in L_{x,t}^{2}
and
\rho\in L_{x,t}^{q+m-1+\frac{2q}{d}}
from (
4.74
), we observe via
\frac{d(q+m-1)+q}{d(q+m-1)+2q}=\frac{d(q+m-1)}{2d(q+m-1)+4q}+\frac{1}{2}
that

\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\in L_{x,t}^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{d(q+m-1)+q}}.

(4.82)

It is clear that
\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\in L^{1}_{x,t}
.
Also, Remark
4.16
and similar computations for
V\in\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}
yield

V\rho^{q}\in L_{x,t}^{\frac{d(m+q-1)+2q}{d(m+q-1)+q}}\quad\text{and}\quad\rho^{q}\,\nabla\cdot V\in L^{1}_{x,t}.

(4.83)

Furthermore, it holds that
V\nabla\rho^{q}\in L^{1}_{x,t}
using
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
and (
4.79
).
Hence, owing to (
4.82
) and (
4.83
), it follows that

\partial_{t}\rho^{q}\in W_{x}^{-1,\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{d(q+m-1)+q}}L_{t}^{1}
in (
4.80
) or (
4.81
). Furthermore,
\partial_{t}\rho^{q}
satisfies (
4.76
).

(ii)
We follow the above proof to estimate
\partial_{t}\rho^{q}
. However, to estimate
\nabla\rho^{q}
in (
4.77
), we employ (
4.56
) for
1<r\leq q
and observe that

\nabla\rho^{q}=\frac{2q}{m+r-1}\rho^{q-\frac{m+r-1}{2}}\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+r-1}{2}}\in L_{x,t}^{\frac{2d(q+m-1)+4q}{d(3q-r)+2q}}

(4.84)

as long as
q\geq\frac{r+m-1}{2}>\frac{m}{2}
for
r\in(1,q]
. Then (
4.84
) holds
via
\frac{2qd-d(r+m-1)}{2d(q+m-1)+4q}+\frac{1}{2}=\frac{d(3q-r)+4q}{2d(q+m-1)+4q}
because of
\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+r-1}{2}}\in L^{2}_{x,t}
and
\rho\in L_{x,t}^{q+m-1+\frac{2q}{d}}
from (
4.56
). This completes the proof.

5
Existence of regular solutions

We recall porous medium equations with divergence form of drifts

\partial_{t}\rho=\nabla\cdot(\nabla\rho^{m}-V\rho)\quad\text{for}\quad\rho(\cdot,0)=\rho_{0}.\\

(5.1)

In this section, we construct a regular solution of (
5.1
) when the initial data
\rho_{0}
and the vector field
V
are smooth enough.
For this, we exploit a splitting method in the Wasserstein space
\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
and it turns out that our soultion is in the class of ablsolutely continuous curves in
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
For carrying the splitting method, it requires a priori estimates, propagation of compact support, and Hölder continuity of the following form of homogeneous PME

\partial_{t}\varrho=\Delta\varrho^{m}\quad\text{ for }\quad\varrho(\cdot,0)=\varrho_{0}.

(5.2)

First, we deliver a priori estimates and compact supports of solutions of (
5.2
) in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.1

Suppose that
\varrho
is a nonnegative
L^{q}
-weak solution of (
5.2
) in Definition
2.1
.

(i)

Let
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{0}(1+\log\varrho_{0})\,dx<\infty
. Then there exists a positive constant
c=c(m,d,p)
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho(\cdot,T)\log\varrho(\cdot,T)\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\frac{\nabla\varrho^{m}}{\varrho}\right|^{p}\varrho\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{0}\log\varrho_{0}\,dx+cT\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{0}\,dx.

(5.3)

Moreover, there exists
T^{\ast}=T^{\ast}(m,d,p)
such that, for any positive integer
l=1,2,3,\cdots
,

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho(\cdot,lT^{\ast})\log\varrho(\cdot,lT^{\ast})\,dx+\int_{(l-1)T^{\ast}}^{lT^{\ast}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\frac{\nabla\varrho^{m}}{\varrho}\right|^{p}\varrho\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[\varrho_{0}\log\varrho_{0}+\varrho_{0}\right]\,dx.

(5.4)

(ii)

Let
\varrho_{0}\in L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
for
q>1
. Then, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho^{q}(\cdot,T)\,dx+\frac{4mq(q-1)}{(m+q-1)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho_{0}^{q}\,dx.

(5.5)

(iii)

If
\varrho_{0}
is compactly supported, then
\varrho(t)
is compactly supported for all
t>0
.
More precisely, suppose there exists
R_{0}>0
such that
\supp(\varrho_{0})\subset B_{R_{0}}(0):=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:|x|\leq R_{0}\}
. Then, for all
t>0
, we have

\supp\left(\varrho(t)\right)\subset B_{R(t)}(0),\quad R(t):=R_{0}\left(1+\beta t\right),~~\beta:=\frac{1}{d(m-1)+2}.

(5.6)

Proof.

We refer
[
53
, Proposition 5.12]
which gives (
5.5
) directly.
Now we prove (
5.3
) and (
5.4
).
First, we observe the following by testing
\log\rho
to (
A.1
) that

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho\log\varrho\,dx+\frac{4}{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\nabla\varrho^{m/2}\right|^{2}\,dx=0.

(5.7)

To above estimate, we take the integration in
t\in[0,T]
and the combination of
p
-th moment estimate (
4.3
) with
2\varepsilon=4/m
yields (
5.3
).

To obtain (
5.4
), we begin from (
5.3
) for
T^{\ast}
instead of
T
. Then we fix
T^{\ast}=T^{\ast}(m,d,p)
small enough such that
cT^{\ast}\leq 1
.
For any positive integer
l
, let us set the interval
[(l-1)T^{\ast},lT^{\ast}]
. In each interval, we apply (
5.3
) to obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho(\cdot,lT^{\ast})\log\varrho(\cdot,lT^{\ast})\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{(l-1)T^{\ast}}^{lT^{\ast}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\frac{\nabla\varrho^{m}}{\varrho}\right|^{p}\varrho\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left[\varrho(\cdot,(l-1)T^{\ast}\log\varrho(\cdot,(l-1)T^{\ast})+\varrho(\cdot,(l-1)T^{\ast})\right]\,dx.

Note that
\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho\log\varrho\,dx\leq 0
that is trivially deduced from (
5.7
).
Then (
5.4
) is obtained because of the decreasing property of
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varrho(\cdot,t)\log\varrho(\cdot,t)\,dx
and the mass conservation property.

To obtain (
5.6
), we exploit Proposition 9.18 of
[
53
]
and have

\supp\left(\varrho(t)\right)\subset B_{R(t)}(0),\quad R(t):=C\left(\left(\frac{R_{0}}{C}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}+t\right)^{\beta},~~\beta:=\frac{1}{d(m-1)+2},

for some constant
C>0
which is independent of
\varrho_{0}
. Since
0<\beta\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we note that the function
f(x):=x^{\beta}
is concave and hence we have
f(x+t)\leq f(x)+f^{\prime}(x)t
for any
x,\,t>0
. That is,

\displaystyle R(t)

\displaystyle=C\left(\left(\frac{R_{0}}{C}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}+t\right)^{\beta}\leq C\left(\frac{R_{0}}{C}+\beta\left(\frac{R_{0}}{C}\right)^{\frac{\beta-1}{\beta}}t\right).

(5.8)

Without loss of generality, we may choose
R_{0}
big so that
R_{0}>C
. Then, from (
5.8
), we have

R(t)\leq\left(R_{0}+\beta R_{0}t\right),

which completes the proof.

Second, we deliver Hölder continuity of a bounded nonnegative solution of (
5.2
) when the initial data is also Hölder continuous (say that
\varrho_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha_{0}}
for some
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
). It is well known that bounded
L^{q}
-weak solutions of (
5.2
) are Hölder continuous up to the initial boundary with quantitatively determined Hölder exponents depending on given data (in particular with (
5.2
), depending only upon
m
,
d
, and
\alpha_{0}
). For example, we refer
[
3
,
4
,
11
,
12
,
23
,
22
,
25
,
26
,
24
,
52
]
.
Here, we introduce the following theorem that the Hölder exponent is more clearly specified as the minimum of intial Hölder exponent
\alpha_{0}
and the interior Hölder exponent
\alpha^{\ast}=\alpha^{\ast}(m,d)
(see Theorem
A.1
for interior Hölder continuity). This result is probably well-known to experts but we could not find it in the literature. See Appendix
A
for more details of proof.
By the aid of Theorem
5.2
, we are able to carry the splitting method because any weak solution of (
5.2
) keeps the same level of regularity at any time.

Theorem 5.2

Let
\varrho
be a bounded nonnegative weak solution of (
5.2
) where the nonnegative initial data
\varrho_{0}
is bounded and Hölder continuous with the exponent
\alpha_{0}\in(0,1)
. Then there exists
\alpha^{\ast}=\alpha^{\ast}(m,d)\in(0,1)
such that
\varrho
is uniformly Hölder continuous on
Q_{T}
with the exponent
\tilde{\alpha}=\min\{\alpha_{0},\alpha^{\ast}\}
; that is,
\|\varrho\|_{\mathcal{C}^{\tilde{\alpha}}(Q_{T})}\leq\gamma
where
\gamma=\gamma(m,d,\|\varrho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})>1
.

Our main result in this section reads as follow.

Proposition 5.3

Let
\tilde{\alpha}\in(0,1)
be the constant in Theorem
5.2
.
Assume
\alpha\in(0,\tilde{\alpha}]
and

{\rho}_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\quad\mbox{and}\quad V\in L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(Q_{T}).

Then there exists an absolutely continuous curve
{\rho}\in AC(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
which is a solution of (
5.1
)
in the sense of distributions and satisfies the followings:

(i)

For all
0\leq s<t\leq T
, we have

W_{2}(\rho(s),\rho(t))\leq C\sqrt{t-s}+\int_{s}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau

(5.9)

where the constant
C=C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right)
.

(ii)

For all
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\left|\rho(x,t)-\rho(y,t)\right|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha},

where the constant
C=C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;C^{1,\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right)
.

(iii)

\bullet
For
q>1

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dt\leq C,

(5.10)

where the constant
C=C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m+q-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},~~\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right)
.

\bullet
For
q=1

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dt\leq C,

(5.11)

where the constant
C=C\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{2}\,dx,\,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right)
.

(iv)

For all
t\in[0,T]
and
q\in[1,\infty]
, we have

\|\rho(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}e^{\frac{q-1}{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau},

(5.12)

where
\frac{q-1}{q}=1
if
q=\infty
.

(v)

If
\rho_{0}
is compactly supported, then
\rho
is also compactly supported in space and time.
More precisely, suppose there exists
R_{0}>0
such that
\supp(\varrho_{0})\subset B_{R_{0}}(0):=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\left|x\right|\leq R_{0}\}
. Then, for all
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\supp\left(\rho(t)\right)\subset B_{R_{T}}(0),\quad R_{T}:=e^{\beta T}\left(R_{0}+\int_{0}^{T}\|v(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt\right),\quad\beta:=\frac{1}{d(m-1)+2}.

(5.13)

5.1
Splitting method

In this subsection, we introduce the splitting method and construct two sequence of curves in

\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
which are approximate solutions of (
5.1
) in
Q_{T}
,

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\rho=\nabla\cdot(\nabla\rho^{m}-V\rho),\\
\rho(\cdot,0)=\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).\end{cases}

For each
n\in\mathbb{N},
we define approximated solutions

\varrho_{n},~\rho_{n}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as follows;

•

For
t\in[0,\frac{T}{n}]
, we define
\varrho_{n}:[0,\frac{T}{n}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as the solution of

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\varrho_{n}=\Delta(\varrho_{n})^{m},\\
\varrho_{n}(\cdot,0)=\rho_{0}.\end{cases}

We also define
\rho_{n}:[0,\frac{T}{n}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as follows

\rho_{n}(t)=\Psi(t;0,\varrho_{n}(t)),\quad\forall~t\in[0,\frac{T}{n}].

Here, we recall (
3.10
) for the definition of
\Psi
. Note that we assume
V\in L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(Q_{T})
in Proposition
5.3
, and hence this flow map
\Psi
used for the proof of Proposition

5.3
is well defined.

•

For
t\in(\frac{T}{n},\frac{2T}{n}]
, we define
\varrho_{n}:(\frac{T}{n},\frac{2T}{n}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as the solution of

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\varrho_{n}=\Delta(\varrho_{n})^{m},\\
\varrho_{n}(\cdot,\frac{T}{n})=\rho_{n}(\frac{T}{n}).\end{cases}

We also define
\rho_{n}:(\frac{T}{n},\frac{2T}{n}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as follows

\rho_{n}(t)=\Psi(t;T/n,\varrho_{n}(t)),\quad\forall~t\in(\frac{T}{n},\frac{2T}{n}].

•

In general, for each
i=1,\ldots,n-1
and
t\in(\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}]
, we define
\varrho_{n}:(\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as the solution of

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\varrho_{n}=\Delta(\varrho_{n})^{m},\\
\varrho_{n}(\cdot,\frac{iT}{n})=\rho_{n}(\frac{iT}{n}).\end{cases}

We also define
\rho_{n}:(\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
as follows

\rho_{n}(t)=\Psi(t;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)),\quad\forall~t\in(\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}].

Now, we investigate some properties useful for the proof of Proposition
5.3
.

Lemma 5.4

Let
V\in L^{1}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
Suppose that
\rho_{n},\varrho_{n}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are curves defined as above with the initial data
\varrho_{n}(0),\rho_{n}(0):=\rho_{0}
.
Then, these curves satisfy the following properties;

(i)

For all
t\in[0,T],
we have

\|\varrho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}},\ \|\rho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}e^{\frac{q-1}{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau},\qquad\forall~q\geq 1.

(5.14)

(ii)

For all
s,t\in[0,T],
we have

\displaystyle W_{2}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{t-s}+\int_{s}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau,

(5.15)

where
C=C(\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})})

(iii)

For all
t\in[0,T],
we have

\displaystyle W_{2}(\rho_{n}(t),{\varrho}_{n}(t))\leq\max_{\{i=0,1,\dots,n\}}\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau.

(5.16)

(iv)

Suppose there exists
R_{0}>0
such that
\supp(\varrho_{0})\subset B_{R_{0}}(0)
. Then, for all
t\in[0,T]
, we have

\supp\left(\rho_{n}(t)\right)\subset B_{R_{n,T}}(0),\quad R_{n,T}:=R_{0}\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)^{n}+\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)^{n-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt,

(5.17)

where
\beta:=\frac{1}{d(m-1)+2}
.

Proof.

(i)
When
q=1
, (
5.14
) is true because of the mass conservation property of
\rho
and
\varrho
. So we only prove for case
q>1
.
Let
t\in\left[0,\frac{T}{n}\right]
be given. From Lemma
5.1

(ii)
, we have

\|\varrho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\qquad\forall~q>1.

(5.18)

We combine (
3.17
) and (
5.18
) to get

\|\rho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|\varrho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}~e^{\frac{q-1}{q}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}~e^{\frac{q-1}{q}\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}.

(5.19)

Similarly, if
t\in(\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}]
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
, then we apply (
5.18
) and
(
5.19
) inductively to get

\|\varrho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}~e^{\frac{q-1}{q}\int_{0}^{\frac{iT}{n}}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau},

and

\|\rho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}~e^{\frac{q-1}{q}\int_{0}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau},

which prove (
5.14
).

(ii)
Now, we prove
AC
-curve property of
\rho_{n}
. Let
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
be given.

\bullet
First, we assume
s,t\in[\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}]
for some
0\leq i\leq n-1
. From the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle W_{2}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq W_{2}(\rho_{n}(s),\Psi(s;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)))+W_{2}(\Psi(s;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)),\rho_{n}(t))

(5.20)

\displaystyle=W_{2}(\Psi(s;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(s)),\Psi(s;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)))+W_{2}(\Psi(s;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)),\Psi(t;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)))

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int_{iT/n}^{s}\mbox{Lip}(v_{\tau})\,d\tau}W_{2}(\varrho_{n}(s),\varrho_{n}(t))+W_{2}(\Psi(s;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)),\Psi(t;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t))),

where we use (
3.14
) to get the last inequality. We note that Lemma
3.11
gives us

\displaystyle W_{2}({\varrho}_{n}(s),{\varrho}_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}~\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}~\,d\tau.

(5.21)

Next, we estimate the second term as follows

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(\Psi(s;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)),\Psi(t;iT/n,\varrho_{n}(t)))

\displaystyle=W_{2}^{2}(\psi(s;iT/n,\cdot)_{\#}{\varrho}_{n}(t),\psi(t;iT/n,\cdot)_{\#}{\varrho}_{n}(t))

(5.22)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\psi(s;iT/n,x)-\psi(t;iT/n,x)|^{2}\varrho_{n}(x,t)\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{s}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\right)^{2}\varrho_{n}(x,t)\,dx

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{s}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\Big)^{2}.

Finally, we combine (
5.20
), (
5.21
) and (
5.22
) to get

\displaystyle W_{2}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int_{iT/n}^{s}\mbox{Lip}(V(\tau))\,d\tau}\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}~\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}~\,d\tau+\int_{s}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau.

(5.23)

\bullet
Now, we consider the general case
s\in[\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}]
and
t\in[\frac{(i+k)T}{n},\frac{(i+k+1)T}{n}]
.
Triangle inequality gives us

\displaystyle W_{2}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq W_{2}\big(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}({(i+1)T/n})\big)+\sum_{j=i+1}^{i+k-1}W_{2}\big(\rho_{n}({jT/n}),\rho_{n}({(j+1)T/n})\big)

(5.24)

\displaystyle+W_{2}\big(\rho_{n}({(i+k)T/n}),\rho_{n}(t)\big).

We apply (
5.23
) to each term in (
5.24
), and get

\displaystyle W_{2}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq e^{\int_{0}^{T}\mbox{Lip}(V(\tau))\,d\tau}\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}~\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}~\,d\tau+\int_{s}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau.

(5.25)

We note that

\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}~\,dx\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}~\,d\tau\leq\sqrt{t-s}\Big(\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}~\,dx~\,d\tau\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(5.26)

Now, we estimate the right hand side of (
5.26
) as follows

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}\,dx\,d\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}\,dx\,d\tau+\sum_{j=i+1}^{i+k-1}\int_{\frac{jT}{n}}^{\frac{(j+1)T}{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}\,dx\,d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{\frac{(i+k)T}{n}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}\,dx\,d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{m-1}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(s)\big]^{m}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}((i+1)T/n)\big]^{m}\,dx\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m-1}\sum_{j=i+1}^{i+k-1}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\rho_{n}(jT/n)\big]^{m}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}((j+1)T/n)\big]^{m}\,dx\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m-1}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\rho_{n}((i+k)T/n)\big]^{m}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(t)\big]^{m}\,dx\Big),

where we exploit (
5.5
) for the inequality.
Due to (
3.17
), we have

\displaystyle(m-1)\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|\nabla\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}|^{2}}{\varrho_{n}}~\,dx\,d\tau

(5.27)

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(s)\big]^{m}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}((i+1)T/n)\big]^{m}\,dx\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=i+1}^{i+k-1}\Big(e^{(m-1)\int_{\frac{jT}{n}}^{\frac{(j+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(jT/n)\big]^{m}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}((j+1)T/n)\big]^{m}\,dx\Big)

\displaystyle+\Big(e^{(m-1)\int_{(i+k)T/n}^{t}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}((i+k)T/n)\big]^{m}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(t)\big]^{m}\,dx\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(s)\big]^{m}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(t)\big]^{m}\,dx\Big)+\sum_{j=i+1}^{i+k-1}\Big(e^{(m-1)\int_{\frac{jT}{n}}^{\frac{(j+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}-1\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(jT/n)\big]^{m}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(s)\big]^{m}\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(t)\big]^{m}\,dx\Big)

\displaystyle+e^{(m-1)\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\sum_{j=i+1}^{i+k-1}\Big((m-1)\int_{\frac{jT}{n}}^{\frac{(j+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\Big)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(jT/n)\big]^{m}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\left[(m-1)e^{(m-1)\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau+1\right]\sup_{0\leq\tau\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big[\varrho_{n}(\tau)\big]^{m}\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\left[(m-1)e^{(m-1)\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau+1\right]e^{(m-1)\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{\varrho}_{0}^{m}\,dx.

Combining (
5.25
)-(
5.27
), we have

\displaystyle W_{2}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{t-s}+\int_{s}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau,

where the constant
C=C(\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau,\|{\rho}_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})})
. This concludes the proof of (
5.15
).

(iii)
Next, we estimate the distance between
\varrho_{n}
and
\rho_{n}
. For any
t\in[0,T]
, there exists
i
such that

t\in[\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}]
. Then

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}({\varrho}_{n}(t),\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle=W_{2}^{2}({\varrho}_{n}(t),\Psi(t;iT/n,\cdot)_{\#}{\varrho}_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}|x-\psi(t;iT/n,x)|^{2}\varrho_{n}(x,t)~\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\Big(\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\Big)^{2}\varrho_{n}(x,t)~\,dx

\displaystyle=\Big(\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\Big)^{2},

which gives us (
5.16
).

(iv)

Let
t\in\left[0,\frac{T}{n}\right]
be given. From (
5.6
), we have

\supp(\varrho_{n}(t))\subset B_{R(t)}(0),\quad R(t):=R_{0}(1+\beta t).

Next, it is easy to check

\displaystyle\supp(\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\subset\supp(\varrho_{n}(t))+B_{D(t)}(0),
