\displaystyle D(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\subset B_{R(t)}(0),

\displaystyle R(t):=R_{0}(1+\beta t)+\int_{0}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,d\tau

\displaystyle\subset B_{R_{1}}(0),

\displaystyle R_{1}:=R_{0}\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)+\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt.

Similarly, for
t\in\left[\frac{T}{n},\frac{2T}{n}\right]
, we have

\supp(\varrho_{n}(t))\subset B_{R(t)}(0),\qquad R(t):=R_{1}\left(1+\beta\big(t-\frac{T}{n}\big)\right),

and

\displaystyle\supp(\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\subset B_{R_{2}}(0),\qquad R_{2}:=R_{1}\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)+\int_{\frac{T}{n}}^{\frac{2T}{n}}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt.

Note that

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=R_{0}\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)^{2}+\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt+\int_{\frac{T}{n}}^{\frac{2T}{n}}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq R_{0}\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)^{2}+\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)\int_{0}^{\frac{2T}{n}}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt.

By continuing the process, for
t\in\left[\frac{(i-1)T}{n},\frac{iT}{n}\right]
and
i=1,2,\dots,n
, we have

\supp(\rho_{n}(t))\subset B_{R_{i}}(0),\quad R_{i}:=R_{0}\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)^{i}+\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)^{i-1}\int_{0}^{\frac{iT}{n}}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt.

Since
R_{i}
is increasing w.r.t
i
, for any
t\in[0,T],
we have
\supp(\rho_{n}(t))\subset B_{R_{n}}(0)
which gives (
5.17
). This completes the proof.

Lemma 5.5

Let
V\in L^{1}(0,T:W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))\cap C^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
and
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\cap\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
Suppose that
\rho_{n},\varrho_{n}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are curves defined as in Lemma

5.4
with the initial data

\varrho_{n}(0),\rho_{n}(0):=\rho_{0}
. Then, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla(\rho_{n})^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dt\leq C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m+q-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},~\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T:W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right),\quad\forall\ q>1.

(5.28)

Proof.

For notational convenience, we use
\rho
and
\varrho
instead of
\rho_{n}
and
\varrho_{n}
, respectively.
First, we note from (
5.14
)

\displaystyle\|\varrho(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}~e^{\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau},\quad\forall~t\in[0,T].

(5.29)

Next, (
5.5
) gives us

\|\varrho(T/n)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}+\frac{4q}{m+q-1}\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,dt=\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q},

and we have from (
3.18
)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+q+3)\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}

(5.30)

\displaystyle\times\left\{\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\times\left(\frac{m+q-1}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla^{2}V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\right)^{2}\right\}.

Combining the fact
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}=1
and (
5.29
) with (
5.30
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+q+3)\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left\{\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C\right\},

(5.31)

where the constant
C=C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m+q-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},~\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right)
. This gives us

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+q+3)\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left\{\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C\frac{T}{n}\right\}.

(5.32)

Similarly, for all
i=1,2,\dots,n-1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\varrho(\frac{(i+1)}{n}T)\right\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}

\displaystyle+\frac{4q}{m+q-1}\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,d\tau=\left\|\rho(\frac{iT}{n})\right\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}

(5.33)

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\varrho(\frac{(i-1)}{n}T)\right\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}~e^{(q-1)\int_{\frac{(i-1)T}{n}}^{\frac{iT}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau},

where we exploit (
3.17
) in the inequality, and

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)}{n}T}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+q+3)\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left\{\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C\frac{T}{n}\right\}.

(5.34)

From (
5.31
) and (
5.33
), we have

\displaystyle\|\varrho(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}+\frac{4q}{m+q-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,d\tau

(5.35)

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho(0)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\|\varrho(iT/n)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}~\left(e^{(q-1)\int_{\frac{(i-1)T}{n}}^{\frac{iT}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}-1\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|\rho(0)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\|\varrho(iT/n)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}~\left((q-1)\left[\int_{\frac{(i-1)T}{n}}^{\frac{iT}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\right]~e^{(q-1)\int_{\frac{(i-1)T}{n}}^{\frac{iT}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\right).

From (
5.32
) and (
5.34
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+q+3)\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+CT\right).

(5.36)

Finally we combine (
5.35
) and (
5.36
) and get

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\leq e^{(m+2q+2)\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\times\left[\frac{(m+q-1)}{4q}\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{q}+\max_{i}\|\varrho(\frac{iT}{n})\|_{L^{q}_{x}}^{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}d\tau\right)+CT\right],

which concludes (
5.28
).

Lemma 5.6

Let
V\in L^{1}(0,T:W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))\cap\mathcal{C}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
and

\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{C}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Suppose that
\rho_{n},\varrho_{n}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are curves defined as in Lemma

5.4
with the initial data

\varrho_{n}(0),\rho_{n}(0):=\rho_{0}
. Then, we have

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla(\rho_{n})^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}dt\leq C,

(5.37)

where
C=C(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{2}\,dx,\,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}).

Proof.

For notational convenience, we use
\rho
and
\varrho
instead of
\rho_{n}
and
\varrho_{n}
, respectively.
First, we note that (
5.7
) gives us

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(\varrho\log\varrho\right)(\frac{T}{n})\,dx+\frac{4}{m}\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,dt=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx,

(5.38)

and we have from (
3.18
)

\displaystyle\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+4)\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left\{\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+\|\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\times\left(\frac{m}{2}\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla^{2}V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\right)^{2}\right\}.

(5.39)

Combining the fact
\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}=1
and (
5.29
) with (
5.39
), we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+4)\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left\{\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C\right\},

where the constant
C=C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},~\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right)
. This gives us

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+4)\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left\{\int_{0}^{\frac{T}{n}}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C\frac{T}{n}\right\}.

Similarly, for all
i=1,2,\dots,n-1
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(\varrho\log\varrho\right)(\frac{(i+1)T}{n})\,dx+\frac{4}{m}\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,dt

(5.40)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(\rho\log\rho\right)(\frac{iT}{n})\,dx\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(\varrho\log\varrho\right)(\frac{iT}{n})\,dx+\int_{\frac{(i-1)T}{n}}^{\frac{iT}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dt,

where we exploit (
3.13
) and (
3.16
) in the inequality, and

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)}{n}T}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq e^{(m+4)\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left\{\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+C\frac{T}{n}\right\}.

(5.41)

From (
5.38
) and (
5.40
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(\varrho\log\varrho\right)(T)\,dx+\frac{4}{m}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,dt\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx+\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dt,

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{4}{m}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,dt\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx+\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}dt-\int_{\varrho(T)<1}\left(\varrho\log\varrho\right)(T)\,dx.

(5.42)

We recall that there exists a constant
\alpha>0
(independent of
\varrho
) such that

-\int_{\varrho(x)<1}\varrho\log\varrho\,dx\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}|x|^{2}\varrho(x)\,dx+\alpha.

(5.43)

We plug (
5.43
) into (
5.42
), and combine with (
5.41
) to get

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\leq e^{(m+4)\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\varrho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}+CT\right)\leq C,

(5.44)

where
C=C\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))},\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}|x|^{2}\varrho(x,T)\,dx\right).

We note

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}|x|^{2}\varrho(x,T)\,dx\leq 2W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0},\varrho(T))+2\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}|x|^{2}\,dx,

(5.45)

and, from (
5.15
) and (
5.16
), we have

W_{2}(\rho_{0},\varrho(T))\leq C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right).

(5.46)

Finally, we combine (
5.44
), (
5.45
) and (
5.46
) to get

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\leq C(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}|x|^{2}\,dx,\,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}),

which concludes (
5.37
).

Next lemma is similar to Lemma 4.2
[
35
]
. So, we briefly sketch the proof of it (see Lemma 4.2
[
35
]
).

Lemma 5.7

Let
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}^{d}}\times[0,T])
and
\rho_{n},\varrho_{n}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be the curves defined as in Lemma
5.4
.
For any
s,~t\in[0,T]
, we define

\displaystyle E_{n}

\displaystyle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)~\rho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)~\rho_{n}(x,s)\,dx

\displaystyle-\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(\nabla\varphi\cdot V)~\rho_{n}\,dx\,d\tau-\int_{s}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\{\partial_{\tau}\varphi~\varrho_{n}+\Delta\varphi~(\varrho_{n})^{m}\}\,dx\,d\tau.

Then,

\displaystyle|E_{n}|\leq C\frac{T}{n}\|\varphi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\int^{t}_{0}\|V(\tau)\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\longrightarrow 0,\quad as\quad n\rightarrow\infty,

where
C
is the same constant in Lemma
5.4
.

Proof.

First, we suppose
[s,t]\subset\big[\frac{kT}{n},\frac{(k+1)T}{n}\big]
for some
0\leq k\leq n-1
. Then, we have

\mathcal{I}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho_{n}(x,s)\,dx\\
=I+II+III,

(5.47)

where

\begin{gathered}I=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\varrho_{n}(x,t)\,dx,\\
II=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\varrho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\varrho_{n}(x,s)\,dx,\\
III=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\varrho_{n}(x,s)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho_{n}(x,s)\,dx.\end{gathered}

Note that

II=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big[\partial_{\tau}\varphi~\varrho_{n}+\Delta\varphi\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}\big]\,dx\,d\tau.

(5.48)

Following the same estimation in the proof of Lemma 4.2
[
35
]
, we have

I=\int_{kT/n}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\nabla\varphi\cdot V)\rho_{n}\,dx\,d\tau+E_{kT/n,t},

(5.49)

where

\displaystyle|E_{kT/n,t}|\leq C\|\varphi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}\Big[\Big(t-\frac{kT}{n}\Big)+\sqrt{t-\frac{kT}{n}}\,\Big]\int^{t}_{kT/n}\|V\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau.

(5.50)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle-III=\int_{kT/n}^{s}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\nabla\varphi\cdot V)\rho_{n}\,dx\,d\tau+E_{kT/n,s},

(5.51)

with

\displaystyle|E_{kT/n,s}|\leq C\|\varphi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}\Big[\Big(s-\frac{kT}{n}\Big)+\sqrt{s-\frac{kT}{n}}\,\Big]\int^{s}_{kT/n}\|V\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau.

(5.52)

We plug (
5.49
), (
5.48
) and (
5.51
) into (
5.47
) to get

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\nabla\varphi\cdot V)\rho_{n}\,dx\,d\tau+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\big[\partial_{\tau}\varphi~\varrho_{n}+\Delta\varphi\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}\big]\,dx\,d\tau+E_{kT/n,t}+E_{kT/n,s}.

(5.53)

Now, we consider the general case :
s\in\big[\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}\big]
and
t\in\big[\frac{jT}{n},\frac{(j+1)T}{n}\big]
.
For this case, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho_{n}(x,s)\,dx

(5.54)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,jT/n)\rho_{n}(x,jT/n)\,dx

\displaystyle+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,(i+1)T/n)\rho_{n}(x,(i+1)T/n)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho_{n}(x,s)\,dx

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=i+1}^{j-1}\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,(k+1)T/n)\rho_{n}(x,(k+1)T/n)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,kT/n)\rho_{n}(x,kT/n)\,dx\right).

We plug (
5.53
) in each term of (
5.54
), and get

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho_{n}(x,s)\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\nabla\varphi\cdot V)\rho_{n}\,dx\,d\tau+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}[\partial_{\tau}\varphi\varrho_{n}+\Delta\varphi\big(\varrho_{n}\big)^{m}]\,dx\,d\tau

\displaystyle+E_{jT/n,t}+E_{iT/n,s}+E_{iT/n,(i+1)T/n}+\sum_{k=i+1}^{j-1}E_{kT/n,(k+1)T/n}.

By using (
5.50
) and (
5.52
), we estimate the error term as follows,

\displaystyle E_{n}

\displaystyle:=E_{jT/n,t}+E_{iT/n,s}+E_{iT/n,(i+1)T/n}+\sum_{k=i+1}^{j-1}E_{kT/n,(k+1)T/n}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{T}{n}\|\varphi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\int^{t}_{iT/n}\|V\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\longrightarrow 0,

as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Lemma 5.8

Let
\tilde{\alpha}\in(0,1)
be the constant in Theorem
5.2
. Suppose that
\alpha\in(0,\tilde{\alpha}]
and

\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\quad\mbox{and}\quad V\in L^{1}(0,T;{\mathcal{C}}^{1,\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})).

Let
\rho_{n},\varrho_{n}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be curves defined as in Lemma
5.4
.
Then,we have

|\varrho_{n}(x,t)-\varrho_{n}(y,t)|+|\rho_{n}(x,t)-\rho_{n}(y,t)|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha},\qquad\forall~x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},~t\in[0,T],

(5.55)

where
C(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,d\tau)
.

Proof.
First, we suppose
t\in\left[0,\frac{T}{n}\right]
. Due to Theorem
5.2
, it holds

|\varrho_{n}(x,t)-\varrho_{n}(y,t)|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha},\quad\forall~x,~y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

(5.56)

where
C=C(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})})
.
Next, due to Lemma
3.19
, we have

|\rho_{n}(x,t)-\rho_{n}(y,t)|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha},\quad\forall~x,~y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

(5.57)

where
C=C\left(\text{sup}_{t\in\left[0,\frac{T}{n}\right]}\|\varrho_{n}(t)\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\int_{0}^{T/n}\|\nabla V\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,d\tau\right)
.

We combine (
5.56
) and (
5.57
), and exploit (
5.14
) for
q=\infty
to conclude

|\varrho_{n}(x,t)-\varrho_{n}(y,t)|+|\rho_{n}(x,t)-\rho_{n}(y,t)|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha},\qquad\forall~x,~y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

(5.58)

where
C(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},~\int_{0}^{T/n}\|\nabla V\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,dt)
.

For the general case
t\in\left[\frac{iT}{n},\frac{(i+1)T}{n}\right],~i=1,2,\dots,n-1
, we repeat what we did in the first step and get

|\varrho_{n}(x,t)-\varrho_{n}(y,t)|+|\rho_{n}(x,t)-\rho_{n}(y,t)|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha},\qquad\forall~x,~y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},

(5.59)

where
C(\|\rho(iT/n)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}},\int_{iT/n}^{(i+1)T/n}\|\nabla V\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\alpha}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\,d\tau)
. We combine
(
5.58
) and (
5.59
) with (
5.14
) for
q=\infty
, and conclude (
5.55
)
which completes the proof.

5.2
Proof of Proposition
5.3

Here, we prove Proposition
5.3
.
For each
n\in\mathbb{N},
from Lemma
5.7
, there exist curves
\rho_{n},\varrho_{n}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with
\rho_{n}(0)=\varrho_{n}(0)=\rho_{0}
satisfying, for any
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[0,T))
and
s,~t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho_{n}(x,s)\,dx

(5.60)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\nabla\varphi\cdot V)\rho_{n}\,dx\,d\tau+\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left\{\partial_{\tau}\varphi\varrho_{n}+\Delta\varphi(\varrho_{n})^{m}\right\}\,dx\,d\tau+E_{n},

with

\displaystyle|E_{n}|\leq C\frac{T}{n}\|\varphi\|_{{\mathcal{C}}^{2}}e^{C\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau}\int^{T}_{0}\|V\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau\longrightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{ as }\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

(5.61)

Recalling Lemma
5.4
, we know

W_{2}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))\leq C\sqrt{t-s}+\int_{s}^{t}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau

(5.62)

and

\displaystyle W_{2}(\rho_{n}(t),{\varrho}_{n}(t))\leq\max_{\{i=0,1,\dots,n-1\}}\int_{\frac{iT}{n}}^{\frac{(i+1)T}{n}}\|V(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau,

(5.63)

where the constant
C=C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\,\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{1,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))}\right)
.

Due to the fact
\rho_{n}(0)=\rho_{0},\,\forall~n\in\mathbb{N}
and (
5.62
), there exists
M>0
such that

\rho_{n}(t)\in B_{M},\quad\forall~t\in[0,T],~n\in\mathbb{N},

where
B_{M}:=\{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})|W_{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\leq M\}
is a narrowly compact subset of
\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
.
Furthermore, the estimate in (
5.62
) says that the sequence of curves
\rho_{n}:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are
equi-continuous. Hence, from Lemma
3.13
, there exist a subsequence (by abusing notation)

\rho_{n}
and a limit curve
\rho:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that

\rho_{n}(t)~~\mbox{ narrowly converges to}~~\rho(t),\qquad\text{for all}~~t\in[0,T].

Due to (
5.63
), we note that
\varrho_{n}(t)
also narrowly converges to
\rho(t),\,\forall~t\in[0,T]
.
This implies

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho_{n}(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho_{n}(x,s)\,dx\longrightarrow\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho(x,s)\,dx,

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left[(\nabla\varphi\cdot V)\rho_{n}+\partial_{\tau}\varphi\varrho_{n}\right]\,dx\,d\tau\longrightarrow\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left[(\nabla\varphi\cdot V)+\partial_{\tau}\varphi\right]\rho\,dx\,d\tau,

as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Furthermore, from Lemma
5.8
,
\rho_{n}
and
\varrho_{n}
are equi-continuous with respect to the space variable.
We combine this equi-continuity with the uniform bound (
5.14
) to claim that
\rho_{n}
and
\varrho_{n}
actually pointwise converge to
\rho
.
Hence, again from Lemma
5.8
, we have

\left|\rho(x,t)-\rho(y,t)\right|\leq C|x-y|^{\alpha},\quad\forall~x,~y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\,t\in[0,T].

Next, we note

\|\varrho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}},~\|\rho_{n}(t)\|_{L^{q}_{x}}\leq\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}e^{\frac{q-1}{q}\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\cdot V\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,d\tau},\quad\forall~t\in[0,T],

(5.64)

coming from (
5.14
) and the pointwise convergence of
(\varrho_{n})^{m}
. Exploiting the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem, we have

\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\varrho_{n})^{m}\,\Delta\varphi\,dx\,d\tau\longrightarrow\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho^{m}\,\Delta\varphi\,dx\,d\tau.

Finally, we exploit the estimation (
5.60
) and (
5.61
) and get

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,t)\rho(x,t)\,dx-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(x,s)\rho(x,s)\,dx=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left[\left(\partial_{\tau}\varphi+V\cdot\nabla\varphi\right){\rho}+\rho^{m}\Delta\varphi\right]\,dx\,d\tau.

From the lower semi-continuity of Wasserstein distance w.r.t the narrow convergence (refer
[
1
, Lemma 7.1.4]
) and (
5.62
), we have (
5.9
).
We also see that the estimation (
5.64
) implies (
5.12
). Note that (
5.28
) gives us

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,dt\leq C,

(5.65)

where the constant
C=C\left(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m+q-1}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\,\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\right)
. This implies that
\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}
(up to a subsequence)
has a weak limit
\eta\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[0,T])
and (
5.65
) gives us

\iint_{Q_{T}}|\eta(x,t)|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C.

(5.66)

Since
\rho_{n}
pointwise converges to
\rho
, we conclude
\eta=\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}
and (
5.10
) follows from (
5.66
). Actually, we note that the whole sequence
\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}
weakly converges to
\nabla\rho^{\frac{m+q-1}{2}}
in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[0,T])
.
Similarly, (
5.37
) gives us

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}}^{2}\,dt\leq C,

(5.67)

where
C=C\left(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{2}\,dx,\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{m}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})},\|V\|_{L^{1}(0,T;W^{2,\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}\right)
, and
we can get (
5.11
) from (
5.67
).

To obtain (
5.13
), we exploit

\displaystyle\supp(\rho(t))

\displaystyle\subset\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}(\supp(\rho_{n}(t))),

\displaystyle\forall~t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\subset\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}B_{R_{n,T}}(0),

\displaystyle R_{n,T}:=R_{0}\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)^{n}+\left(1+\beta\frac{T}{n}\right)^{n-1}\int_{0}^{T}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt

\displaystyle\subset B_{R_{T}}(0),

\displaystyle R_{T}:=R_{0}e^{\beta T}+e^{\beta T}\int_{0}^{T}\|V(t)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\,dt,

where we use (
5.17
) in the second line. This completes the proof.

6
Existence of weak solutions

In this section, we show the existence of
L^{q}
-weak solutions in Definition
2.1
.

6.1
Existence for case:
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx<\infty

Here we provide proofs of theorems in Section
2.1.1
: Theorem
2.3
,
2.4
, and
2.5
.

6.1.1
Proof of Theorem
2.3

Proof of (i).
Let
1<p\leq{\lambda_{1}}
. Suppose
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
with
\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx<\infty
and

V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
satisfies (
2.4
).
By exploiting mollification, we may choose a sequence of vector fields
V_{n}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(Q_{T})

such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}=0.

(6.1)

Using truncation, mollification and normalization, we have a sequence of functions

\rho_{0,n}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
satisfying

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}W_{p}(\rho_{0,n},\rho_{0})=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0,n}\log\rho_{0,n}\,dx=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}\,dx.

(6.2)

We note that
\rho_{0,n}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
implies
\rho_{0,n}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
.
Hence, exploiting Proposition
5.3
, we have
\rho_{n}\in AC(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
which is a regular solution of

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\rho_{n}=\nabla\cdot(\nabla(\rho_{n})^{m}-V_{n}\rho_{n}),\\
\rho_{n}(0)=\rho_{0,n}.\end{cases}

(6.3)

Thus,
\rho_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}\left[\partial_{t}\varphi+(V_{n}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\right]\rho_{n}+(\rho_{n})^{m}\Delta\varphi\,dx\,dt=-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\varphi(0)\rho_{0,n}\,dx,

(6.4)

for each
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[0,T))
, and
\rho_{n}
enjoys all properties in
Proposition
5.3
. Especially, we note

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho_{n}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt<\infty.

(6.5)

Now, we prove
\rho_{n}\in AC(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
. For this, we first note that
\rho_{0,n}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
gives us

\rho_{0,n}\in\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. Next, due to the assumption (
2.4
),
we can exploit Proposition
4.5

(i)
and Proposition
4.9

(i)
to have

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho_{n}(|\log\rho_{n}|+\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\{\left|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m}{2}}\right|^{2}+\left(\left|\frac{\nabla\rho_{n}^{m}}{\rho_{n}}\right|^{p}+|V_{n}|^{p}\right)\rho_{n}\}\,dx\,dt<C,

(6.6)

where the constant
C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0,n}\left(\log\rho_{0,n}+\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx\right)
.
Hence, we have
\rho_{n}(t)\in\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
for all
t\in[0,T]
. Actually, due to (
6.1
) and (
6.2
), we may choose the constant

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\left(\log\rho_{0}+\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx\right)
. Now, we rewrite (
6.3
) as follows

\partial_{t}\rho_{n}+\nabla\cdot(w_{n}\rho_{n})=0,\qquad\mbox{where}\quad w_{n}:=-\frac{\nabla(\rho_{n})^{m}}{\rho_{n}}+V_{n}.

Then, (
6.6
) says

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho_{n}(|\log\rho_{n}|+\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx+\int_{0}^{T}\|w_{n}(t)\|_{L^{p}(\rho_{n}(t))}^{p}\,dt<C.

We exploit Lemma
3.11
and (
6.6
) to have
\rho_{n}\in AC(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
and

\displaystyle W_{p}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}\|w_{n}(\tau)\|_{L^{p}(\rho_{n}(\tau))}\,d\tau\leq C(t-s)^{\frac{p-1}{p}},\qquad\forall~0\leq s\leq t\leq T.

(6.7)

Next, from Lemma
3.13
with (
6.7
), we note that there
exists a curve
\rho:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
such that

\rho_{n}(t)\,\mbox{(up to a subsequence) \,narrowly converges to}\,\rho(t)\,\mbox{as}\,n\rightarrow\infty,\quad\forall\ 0\leq t\leq T.

(6.8)

Due to the lower semicontinuity of
p
-Wasserstein distance
W_{p}(\cdot,\cdot)
with respect to the narrow convergence, we have

W_{p}(\rho(s),\rho(t))\leq C(t-s)^{\frac{p-1}{p}},\qquad\forall~0\leq s\leq t\leq T,

(6.9)

which concludes (
2.6
).

Let us prove that the curve
\rho:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
is a solution of (
1.1
). First, we note

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}(V_{n}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt\longrightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}(V\cdot\nabla\varphi)\rho\,dx\,dt,\,~\mbox{as}\ n\rightarrow\infty,

(6.10)

for any
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[0,T))
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}\big[(V_{n}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\rho_{n}-(V\cdot\nabla\varphi)\rho\big]\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle=\iint_{Q_{T}}\nabla\varphi\cdot\big(V_{n}-V\big)\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt+\iint_{Q_{T}}\nabla\varphi\cdot V\left(\rho_{n}-\rho\right)\,dx\,dt=I+II.

Let us first estimate
I
. Due to (
6.6
), we apply Lemma
3.4
and Remark
4.13
to obtain

\|(V_{n}-V)\rho_{n}\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}}\leq\|V_{n}-V\|_{{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{({q}_{1},{q}_{2})}}\|\rho_{n}\|_{L_{x,t}^{r_{1},r_{2}}}\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}.

(6.11)

Hence, we have

|I|\leq\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L_{x,t}^{md+2}}\|(V_{n}-V)\rho_{n}\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}}\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}\longrightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

(6.12)

Also, narrow convergence in (
6.8
) implies

II
converges to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Hence, this with (
6.12
) implies (
6.10
).

Next, we study the convergence of following nonlinear term

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}(\rho_{n})^{m}\Delta\varphi\,dx\,dt\longrightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho^{m}\,\Delta\varphi\,dx\,dt.

(6.13)

Due to (
4.70
) in Remark
4.13
, we have

\displaystyle\|\rho_{n}^{m}\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{md+2}{md}}}

\displaystyle\leq C.

(6.14)

This implies that
\rho_{n}^{m}
(up to a subsequence) has a weak limit
\eta\in L^{\frac{md+2}{md}}\left(\bar{Q}_{T}\right)
. That is

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}(\rho_{n})^{m}\Delta\varphi~\,dx\,dt\longrightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}\eta\Delta\varphi~\,dx\,dt.

(6.15)

Furthermore, we combine Proposition
4.14
with (
6.14
) to have

\|\partial_{t}\rho_{n}\|_{W_{x}^{-1,\frac{md+2}{md+1}}L_{t}^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}}+\|\rho_{n}\|_{W_{x}^{1,\frac{md+2}{d+1}}L_{t}^{\frac{md+2}{d+1}}}\leq C.

By applying Lemma
3.5
, we have
\rho_{n}
(up to a subsequence) which converges in
L^{\frac{md+2}{d+1}}(0,T;L_{\loc}^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
for

r<\frac{d(md+2)}{d(d-m)+(d-2)}
(see Remark
4.15
).
That implies
\rho_{n}
(up to a subsequence) converges almost everywhere in
\bar{Q}_{T}
.
Now, we recall that
\rho_{n}(t)
narrowly converges to
\rho(t)
for any
t\in[0,T]
.
We put these two facts together and conclude
\rho_{n}
converges to
\rho
almost everywhere in
\bar{Q}_{T}
. Hence,
\rho_{n}^{m}
also pointwise converges to
\rho^{m}
.
Combining this with (
6.15
), we conclude (
6.13
).
We plug (
6.8
), (
6.10
), (
6.13
) into (
6.4
), and have (
2.2
).

Next, we prove (
2.5
). From (
6.6
), we have

\|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L_{x,t}^{2}}\leq C.

(6.16)

As before, with the almost everywhere convergence of
\rho_{n}
to
\rho
, this gives us that
\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m}{2}}
weakly converges to
\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}

in
L^{2}(\bar{Q}_{T})
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L_{x,t}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C.

(6.17)

Moreover, (
4.3
) and (
6.17
) give us

\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{p}\rho\,dx\,dt\leq C.

(6.18)

Next, we note

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}|V_{n}|^{p}\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt\longrightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{p}\rho\,dx\,dt,\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

(6.19)

Indeed, we have

\iint_{Q_{T}}\left(|V_{n}|^{p}\rho_{n}-|V|^{p}\rho\right)\,dx\,dt=\iint_{Q_{T}}\left(|V_{n}|^{p}-|V|^{p}\right)\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt+\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{p}\left(\rho_{n}-\rho\right)\,dx\,dt=I+II.

Let us first estimate
I
. We exploit (
4.9
) in Lemma
4.2
to have

\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|V_{n}-V\right|^{{\lambda_{1}}}\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{T}\|V_{n}-V\|_{L_{x}^{q_{1}}}^{{\lambda_{1}}}\|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{\frac{2(1-\theta)}{m}},

where
\theta
and
q_{2}
are defined in (
4.8
) and (
4.10
) for
q=1
, respectively. Notice that
\frac{{\lambda_{1}}}{q_{2}}+\frac{1-\theta}{m}=1
, and hence we have

\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|V_{n}-V\right|^{{\lambda_{1}}}\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt\leq\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{{\lambda_{1}}}\|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{m}{2}}\|_{L_{x,t}^{2}}^{\frac{2(1-\theta)}{m}}\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{{\lambda_{1}}},

(6.20)

where we exploit (
6.16
) in the second inequality. For any
1<p<{\lambda_{1}}
, from (
4.12
), we have

|I|\leq C\||V_{n}-V|^{p}\rho_{n}\|_{L_{x,t}^{1}}\leq C\||V_{n}-V|^{{\lambda_{1}}}\rho_{n}\|_{L_{x,t}^{1}}^{\frac{p}{{\lambda_{1}}}}\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{p}\longrightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

(6.21)

Also, narrow convergence in (
6.8
) implies
II
converges to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. This with (
6.21
) gives us (
6.19
)
which implies

\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{p}\rho\,dx\,dt\leq C.

(6.22)

We remind that the entropy and
p
-th moment are lower semicontinuous with respect to the narrow convergence (refer
[
1
, Remark 9.3.8 and (5.1.15)]
). Hence, from (
6.6
), we have for any
t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho\left(\log\rho+\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{n\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho_{n}\left(\log\rho_{n}+\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx\leq C.

(6.23)

We also remind that there exists a constant
c>0
(independent of
\rho
) such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\min\{\rho\log\rho,~0\}|\,dx\leq c\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx.

(6.24)

Combining (
6.23
) and (
6.24
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho\left(\left|\log\rho\right|+\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx\leq C.

(6.25)

We combine (
6.17
), (
6.18
), (
6.22
) and (
6.25
) to get (
2.5
).
This completes the proof.

Proof of (ii)
: Since
\mathcal{P}_{a}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\subset\mathcal{P}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
if
a\geq b
, we note
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\lambda_{1}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. Then, we exploit the result of
(ii)
with
p={\lambda_{1}}
to
have a solution
\rho
of satisfying (
2.2
), (
2.7
) and (
2.8
) with a constant

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\left(\log\rho_{0}+\langle x\rangle^{{\lambda_{1}}})\,dx\right)
.
Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{{\lambda_{1}}}\,dx\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx
, we may choose the constant

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{0}(\log\rho_{0}+\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx)
. This concludes
(ii)
.

6.1.2
Proof of Theorem
2.4

Similar to the reason in the proof of Theorem
2.3

(ii)
, we only focus on the case
1<p\leq{\lambda_{1}}
.
As in the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we have
\rho_{n}\in AC(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
which is a regular solution of (
6.3
) satisfying (
6.4
) and (
6.5
).

Since
V
holds
\nabla\cdot V=0

and (
2.9
),
V_{n}
is also divergence-free and satisfies (
2.9
) for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then, from Proposition
4.5

(iii)
and Proposition
4.9

(iii)
, we have (
6.6
).

As in the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we exploit the estimate (
6.6
) and note that there
exists a curve
\rho:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
satisfying (
6.8
) and (
6.9
).

We note that Remark
4.13
,
4.15
, and Proposition
4.14
follow from Proposition
4.5
, and also note that Lemma
4.2
holds from
(
6.5
) and the assumption (
2.9
) on
V
. Once Remark
4.13
,
4.15
, Proposition
4.14
and Lemma
4.2
are available, we follow exactly same steps in the proof of Theorem
2.3

to conclude (
2.2
) and (
2.5
) from (
6.4
) and (
6.6
), respectively.

Let us be more precise. In the proof of Theorem
2.3
, (
4.71
) in Remark
4.13
gives (
6.11
)
which plays a key role to have (
6.10
).
Furthermore, for
1<m\leq 2
, Proposition

4.14
and Remark
4.15
with Aubin-Lions lemma give (
6.13
),
which together with (
6.10
) provides crucial ingredients to have (
2.2
).
On the other hand, we exploit (
4.9
) in Lemma
4.2
to have (
6.20
)
and this gives (
6.22
). Having (
6.22
), it follows (
2.5
) and this completes the proof.

6.1.3
Proof of Theorem
2.5

The proof is completed in the exactly same as in Theorem

2.3
or Theorem
2.4
. The only difference is that,
instead of (
6.1
), we choose a sequence of vector
fields
V_{n}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|V_{n}-V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}=0.

And also, in stead of (
6.11
), we have

\|(V_{n}-V)\rho_{n}\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{md+2}{md+1}}}\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,1}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}\longrightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\rightarrow\infty,

(6.26)

where
C=C(\|V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,1}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}},\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(\rho_{0}\log\rho_{0}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p})\,dx)
. Here,
q_{1}:=\frac{d\tilde{q}_{1}}{d-\tilde{q}_{1}},~q_{2}:=\tilde{q}_{2}
.
We exploit (
1.7
) in the second inequality of (
6.26
) because the assumption (
2.10
) guarantees
1<\tilde{q}_{1}<d
(see Remark
1.2

(ii)
).

6.2
Existence for case:
\rho_{0}\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ q>1

Here we prove theorems in Section
2.1.2
: Theorem
2.7
and
2.9
.

6.2.1
Proof of Theorem
2.7

Suppose
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
and
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
satisfies (
2.11
).
By exploiting mollification, we may choose a sequence of vector fields
V_{n}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}=0.

(6.27)

Using truncation, mollification and normalization, we have a sequence of functions

\rho_{0,n}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
satisfying

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\rho_{0,n}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}=0.

(6.28)

As in the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we have
\rho_{n}\in AC(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
a regular solution of (
6.3
) satisfying (
6.4
) and

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{t\}}(\rho_{n})^{q}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt<\infty.

From Proposition
4.6

(i)
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{t\}}(\rho_{n})^{q}\,dx+\frac{2qm(q-1)}{(q+m-1)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(6.29)

where
C=C(\|V_{n}\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0,n}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
.
Due to (
6.27
) and (
6.28
), we may choose

C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
.

From (
6.29
), we note that
\rho_{n}
is bounded in
L^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{x,t}(\bar{Q}_{T})
where
(r_{1},r_{2})
are satisfying (
3.2
).
Hence, there exists a subsequence (by abusing notation)
\rho_{n}
weakly converges to

\rho\in L^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{x,t}(\bar{Q}_{T})
(in particular, it is weak* convergence if
r_{1}=\infty
or
r_{2}=\infty
).

We claim that
\rho
is a solution of (
1.1
). First, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}(V_{n}\cdot\nabla\varphi)\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt\longrightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}(V\cdot\nabla\varphi)\rho\,dx\,dt,~~~\mbox{as}~~n\rightarrow\infty,

(6.30)

for any
\varphi\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[0,T))
. Indeed, we let

\displaystyle I:=\iint_{Q_{T}}\nabla\varphi\cdot\big(V_{n}-V\big)\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt\quad\text{and}\quad II:=\iint_{Q_{T}}\nabla\varphi\cdot\left(V\rho_{n}-V\rho\right)~\,dx\,dt.

From Lemma
4.17
, we have

\displaystyle I

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|(V_{n}-V)\rho_{n}^{q}\nabla\varphi\right|\,dxdt\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|(V_{n}-V)\nabla\varphi\right|\,dxdt\right)^{1-\frac{1}{q}}

(6.31)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|(V_{n}-V)\rho_{n}^{q}\nabla\varphi\right|^{r}\,dxdt\right)^{\frac{1}{qr}}\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\varphi\right|\,dxdt\right)^{\frac{1}{q}(1-\frac{1}{r})}\left(\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|(V_{n}-V)\nabla\varphi\right|\,dxdt\right)^{1-\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varphi)\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{\frac{1}{q}}\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{x,t}}\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{1-\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varphi)\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}\|\rho_{n}\|_{L^{r_{1},r_{2}}_{x,t}}\longrightarrow 0,\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

Also, weak convergence of
\rho_{n}
implies that
II
converges
to
0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. This together with (
6.31
) gives us (
6.30
).

Next, we study the convergence of following nonlinear term

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}(\rho_{n})^{m}\Delta\varphi\,dx\,dt\longrightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}\rho^{m}\Delta\varphi\,dx\,dt.

(6.32)

Due to (
4.74
) in Remark
4.16
, we have

\displaystyle\|\rho_{n}^{m}\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{md}}}

\displaystyle\leq C,

(6.33)

where
C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})
.
This implies
(\rho_{n})^{m}
(up to a subsequence) has a weak limit
\eta\in L^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{md}}(Q_{T})
.
In other words, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}(\rho_{n})^{m}\Delta\varphi\,dx\,dt\longrightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}\eta\Delta\varphi\,dx\,dt.

(6.34)

Furthermore, combining Proposition
4.19
(i)
and (
6.33
), we have

\|\partial_{t}(\rho_{n})^{q}\|_{W_{x}^{-1,\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{d(q+m-1)+q}}L_{t}^{1}}+\|\nabla(\rho_{n})^{q}\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{q(d+1)}}}\leq C.

(6.35)

Combining (
6.35
) and Lemma
3.5
, we have
(\rho_{n})^{q}
(up to a subsequence) which converges in
L^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{q(d+1)}}(0,T;L_{\loc}^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
for

r<p=\frac{d^{2}(q+m+1)+2dq}{q(d^{2}-2)-d(m-1)}
where
p
is defined in (
4.78
), Remark
4.20
.
Hence,
\rho_{n}
and
(\rho_{n})^{q}
also converge almost everywhere in
\bar{Q}_{T}
.
Recalling
\rho_{n}
weakly converges to
\rho
, we can replace
\eta
by
\rho^{m}
in (
6.34
) which gives us (
6.32
).
We plug (
6.30
) and
(
6.32
) into (
6.4
), and get (
2.2
).

Now, we investigate (
2.12
). Using (
6.29
), weak convergence, and following similarly the
proof of Theorem
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho^{q}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dxdt\leq C.

(6.36)

Finally, we combine (
2.2
) and (
6.36
) to complete the proof.

6.2.2
Proof of Theorem
2.9

For the case
(i)
: Similar to the reason in the proof of Theorem
2.3
, if
p>{\lambda_{q}}
then
\rho_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\lambda_{q}}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
. Therefore, the case (b) follows from (a).
Hence, we only consider the case (a) i.e. for
1<p\leq{\lambda_{q}}
.
Assume that
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}
satisfies (
2.13
).
As in the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we have a sequence of vector fields
V_{n}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(Q_{T})
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}=0,

(6.37)

and a sequence of functions
\rho_{0,n}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{c}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})\cap\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
satisfying

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}W_{p}(\rho_{0,n},\rho_{0})=0,\qquad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\|\rho_{0,n}-\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})}=0.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we have
\rho_{n}\in AC(0,T;\mathcal{P}_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))
, a regular solution of (
6.3
) satisfying (
6.4
).

Note that
\frac{2m-1}{m-1}<\frac{q+m-1}{m-1}
for
q>m
. Hence, under the assumption (
2.13
) on
V
,
we note that Proposition
4.6

(i)
and Proposition
4.10

(i)
are available, and we have

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{t\}}\{(\rho_{n})^{q}+\rho_{n}\langle x\rangle^{p}\}\,dx+\iint_{Q_{T}}\{\left|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}+\left(\left|\frac{\nabla\rho_{n}^{m}}{\rho_{n}}\right|^{p}+|V_{n}|^{p}\right)\rho_{n}\}\,dx\,dt\leq C,

(6.38)

where the constant
C=C(\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}},\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\rho_{0}^{q}+\rho_{0}\langle x\rangle^{p}\right)\,dx)
.

As in (
6.7
), we exploit Lemma
3.11
with (
6.38
) and have

W_{p}(\rho_{n}(s),\rho_{n}(t))\leq C(t-s)^{\frac{p-1}{p}},\qquad\forall~0\leq s\leq t\leq T.

Similar to (
6.8
) and (
6.9
), there exists a curve

\rho:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
such that, as
n\rightarrow\infty
(up to subsequence)

\rho_{n}(t)~~\mbox{narrowly converges to}~~\rho(t)\quad\mbox{and}\quad W_{p}(\rho(s),\rho(t))\leq C(t-s)^{\frac{p-1}{p}},\qquad\forall~0\leq s\leq t\leq T.

(6.39)

Furthermore, the curve
\rho:[0,T]\mapsto\mathcal{P}_{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
is a solution of (
1.1
). Indeed, in the proof of Theorem
2.7
, we exploited
Remark
4.16
and Proposition
4.19
(i), and showed following convergence (
6.30
) and (
6.32
).
By plugging (
6.39
), (
6.30
) and
(
6.32
) into (
6.4
), we get (
2.2
).

Next, we investigate (
2.14
). We first note that (
6.38
), (
6.39
) and the lower semicontinuity of
L^{q}
-norm w.r.t
the narrow convergence give us (
6.36
).
Moreover, (
4.3
) and (
6.36
) give us

\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\frac{\nabla\rho^{m}}{\rho}\right|^{p}\rho\,dx\,dt\leq\|\nabla\rho^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\|_{L_{x,t}^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho^{q}\,dx\right)\leq C.

(6.40)

Next, we investigate

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{p}\rho\,dx\,dt\leq C.

(6.41)

First, for the case
1<q\leq m
, we exploit (
4.9
) to have

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|V_{n}-V\right|^{{\lambda_{q}}}\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq c(\|\rho_{n}\|_{L_{x,t}^{q,\infty}})\int_{0}^{T}\|V_{n}-V\|_{L_{x}^{q_{1}}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}\|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\|_{L_{x}^{2}}^{\frac{2(1-\theta)}{q+m-1}}\,dt,

(6.42)

where
\theta
and
q_{2}
are defined in (
4.8
) and (
4.10
), respectively. Notice that
\frac{{\lambda_{q}}}{q_{2}}+\frac{1-\theta}{q+m-1}=1
, and hence we have

\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|V_{n}-V\right|^{{\lambda_{q}}}\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt\leq\|V_{n}-V\|_{L_{x,t}^{q_{1},q_{2}}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}\|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\|_{L_{x,t}^{2}}^{\frac{2(1-\theta)}{q+m-1}}\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{L_{x,t}^{q_{1},q_{2}}}^{{\lambda_{q}}},

(6.43)

where we exploit (
6.38
) in the second inequality.

Second, for the case
q>m
, we note
{\lambda_{q}}=2
. Combining (
6.42
) and (
6.43
), we have

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|V_{n}-V\right|^{{\lambda_{q}}}\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{L_{x,t}^{q_{1},q_{2}}}^{{\lambda_{q}}},

where

C=C(\|\rho_{0}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},~\|V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}})
.
Either
1<q\leq m
or
q>m
, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|V_{n}-V\right|^{{\lambda_{q}}}\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{\mathcal{S}_{m,q}^{(q_{1},q_{2})}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}\longrightarrow 0\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\rightarrow\infty.

(6.44)

Now, as in the proof of Theorem
2.3
, we exploit (
6.44
) and conclude

\displaystyle\iint_{Q_{T}}|V_{n}|^{p}\rho_{n}\,dx\,dt\longrightarrow\iint_{Q_{T}}|V|^{p}\rho\,dx\,dt\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\rightarrow\infty,

which implies (
6.41
).

Since
p
-th moment is lower semicontinuous w.r.t the narrow convergence, (
6.38
) gives us

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times\{t\}}\rho\langle x\rangle^{p}\,dx\leq C.

Finally, we combine (
6.36
), (
6.40
), (
6.41
) and (
6.2.2
) to conclude (
2.14
).

For the case
(ii)
: Let
q>m
and assume
V
satisfies (
2.15
). Then, from Proposition
4.11

(i)
, there exist

q^{\ast}\in(1,q)
and
q_{2}^{\ast}=q_{2}^{\ast}(q_{1})\in[2,q_{2})
such that
V\in\mathcal{S}_{m,q^{\ast}}^{(q_{1},q_{2}^{\ast})}
satisfying (
2.13
).
Hence, the same conclusions hold as in Theorem
2.9

(i)
. This completes the
proof.

6.3
Existence for case:
\nabla\cdot V=0
and
\rho_{0}\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ q>1

Here we prove theorems in Section
2.1.3
: Theorem
2.11
and
2.13
.

6.3.1
Proof of Theorem
2.11

The proof can be completed almost the same way as in the proof of Theorem
2.7
. Let us just point out the differences between the proofs of two theorems.

The first difference is that Proposition
4.6
gives us, due to the divergence-free assumption
\nabla\cdot V=0

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{t\}}(\rho_{n})^{q}\,dx+\frac{qm(q-1)}{(q+m-1)^{2}}\iint_{Q_{T}}\left|\nabla\rho_{n}^{\frac{q+m-1}{2}}\right|^{2}\,dx\,dt\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\rho_{n,0}^{q}\,dx,

instead of (
6.29
). This induces (
2.18
).

The second difference lies in obtaining the estimates of temporal and spatial derivatives when
V
satisfies (
2.17
). For the case
q>\max\{1,\frac{m}{2}\}
, we exploit Proposition
4.19
and have for
r\in(1,q]
,

\|\partial_{t}(\rho_{n})^{q}\|_{W_{x}^{-1,\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{d(q+m-1)+q}}L_{t}^{1}}+\|\nabla(\rho_{n})^{q}\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{q(d+1)}}}\cdot\delta_{\{q\geq m-1\}}+\|\nabla(\rho_{n})^{q}\|_{L_{x,t}^{\frac{2d(q+m-1)+4q}{d(3q-r)+2q}}}\cdot\delta_{\{\frac{m}{2}<q<m-1\}}\leq C.

(6.45)

Hence,

(\rho_{n})^{q}
(up to a subsequence) converges in (see Remark
4.20
)

\begin{cases}L^{\frac{d(q+m-1)+2q}{q(d+1)}}(0,T;L^{r}_{\loc}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))\ \text{ for }\ r<\frac{d^{2}(q+m+1)+2dq}{q(d^{2}-2)-d(m-1)},&\text{ if }q\geq m-1\\
L^{\frac{2d(q+m-1)+4q}{d(3q-r)+2q}}(0,T;L^{r}_{\loc}({\mathbb{R}}^{d}))\ \text{ for }\ r<\frac{2d^{2}(q+m-1)+4dq}{d^{2}(3q-r)-2d(m-1)-4q},&\text{ if }\frac{m}{2}<q<m-1.\end{cases}

This completes the proof.

2.13

For the case

: The proof can be completed almost the same way as in Theorem

(i)
.

), we exploit Proposition

and Proposition

(iii)
instead of

and Proposition

, respectively.

).

and Remark

(ii)
to have (

).

For the case

(iii)
to have constants

.

.

.

. This completes the proof.

2.16

(i)
,

. As expected, the proof can be done similarly to that of Theorem

.

),

V_{n}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\bar{Q}_{T})
satisfying (

).

\leq C\|V_{n}-V\|_{\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{m,q}^{(\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2})}}^{{\lambda_{q}}}\longrightarrow 0\quad\mbox{as}\quad n\rightarrow\infty,

instead of (

) for

and

(see Remark

). Let us also point out that we exploit Proposition

(ii)
and

(ii)

instead of Proposition

and

(i)
, respectively. Lastly, for the strong convergence of
(\rho_{n})^{q}
,
we consider again the two cases separately. That is, for

, we exploit Proposition

(i)
to have (
6.35
). Also, for the case
\frac{m}{2}\leq q<m-1
,
we exploit Proposition

(ii)
and Remark

(ii)
to have (

).

: Let

and assume

). Then, from Proposition

, there exist

and

such that

).
